**Alternatives considered:** *Narrated scroll* — rejected: it is a dashboard with prose on top, the thing the operator ruled out, and it has nowhere to put a decision, so the preference-setting job never happens. *Conversational Q&A* — rejected: canned questions are a worse navigation list, and open input needs an LLM per request, contradicting "reuse what exists", adding per-user API cost on TestFlight, and breaking the deterministic-copy precedent (`trade_narrative.py` is templates by design). *Mount it as an `AnalystGuide` tour* — rejected: that overlay teaches a **control** by cutting a spotlight hole over it, whereas this presents **data** (a cutout over a chart is theatre); it also mounts in `RootNav` above the nav tree, and `useGuide` is coupled to the onboarding tour's lifecycle, which would tie a Trades kill switch to an onboarding flag. *A seventh `TradeFinderModeBar` chip* — rejected on the source's own measurement (`TradeFinderModeBar.tsx:50–58`: five chips already ≈402pt against ≈361pt usable, so the strip is genuinely scrolled and an appended chip "would sit off-screen and never be seen"); the user who most needs this is the least likely to scroll a chip rail. *A separate tab* — ruled out by the operator's framing. *Six per-beat fetches* — rejected: six loading states inside a flow whose value is feeling like one continuous read; a p95 budget of 800 ms is written instead, with the remedy pre-named (cache the power-rankings half, never split).
**Consequences:** The exit of the flow is **a deck reshaped by what the user just agreed to**, not a paragraph — that is what makes #359 answerable. One new dark flag (`trades.team_review`), one new route (`GET /api/league/team-review`), four new client analytics events, and **two existing events extended rather than duplicated** (`outlook_saved.source` and `finder_target_pinned.source` each gain `review`, so the adoption series that already answers this question is not split). No schema change and no migration. A new LLD convention falls out and is load-bearing: **a read-only aggregate route may compose existing service functions but must not import a subsystem behind a different feature flag** — Team Review reads `outlook.league_state` for weekly scores and must never touch `outlook/pipeline.py` or `simulator.py`, or a dark `outlook.odds` and a lit `trades.team_review` stop being clean kill switches. **Three waivers need an operator yes before build** (scope §6) and are not assumed. **Planning ran single-context, not the prescribed dual-agent loop** (standing no-subagent instruction) — the weakness is named in the reconciliation log and an independent read of the API contract is requested before build.
**Status:** **Planned, not built.** Docs only on `claude/team-review-analysis-plan-1f91e3`; no code, and `trades.team_review` is not yet in `config/features.json`.

## D-093 — Team Review Ships Odds-Free; `outlook.odds` Stays Dark, With Lighting Criteria Written as a Gate
**Date:** 2026-08-19 (companion to [D-092](DECISIONS.md); evidence: [`calibration-combined-2026-08-10.md`](../docs/feedback/items/169-outlook-league-summary/calibration-combined-2026-08-10.md) §7–§9)
**Context:** #357 asked for *"here's 6 extra PPG this year which raises your playoff odds to X and championship odds to Y"*. The obvious move was to light `outlook.odds` — dark since it was added, with the backend pipeline, the League-Summary section and the frame-E collapsed strip all fully built (`f27c0f5`) — and hang the new feature off it. `NEXT.md` item 7 has carried the lighting debt for weeks.
**Decision:** **Team Review ships with no odds surface of any kind, and `outlook.odds` stays `false`.** Separately, **forward per-player PPG is cut from scope entirely** and **championship odds are refused outright**. Four reasons against lighting: (1) **platform** — the outlook engine is Sleeper-only (`backend/outlook/league_state.py:295–320` raises `NotImplementedError` for ESPN/MFL/Fleaflicker, route 501s), while Team Review's other five beats work everywhere, so coupling them makes the flow's quality depend on which platform the user linked; (2) **season phase** — `completed_weeks == 0` today, and the combined calibration puts the preseason skill lower CI bound at **+2.9 %** with **2 of 6** backtested league-seasons losing to climatology outright, so lighting inside a new feature makes its first impression carry the model's weakest window; (3) **the owed evidence no longer exists as specified** — NEXT.md item 7 states it as "a Maestro flow + a seeded fixture", and D-056 retired Maestro entirely, so nobody has re-specified it; (4) **nothing in #358/#359 needs odds**, and #357's headline (`title_pct`) is unrenderable by invariant anyway. **Four lighting criteria are written as a future gate** (hld-delta §5): L1 `completed_weeks >= 1` on the operator's league; L2 an explicit non-Sleeper state rather than a 501; L3 evidence re-specified under D-056 (structural `check-outlook-bands.js` pinning the three thresholds and that no client reads `title_pct`, a backend test on `serialize.py`'s `meta.beta` gate at weeks 5 vs 6, a code-walk proof, a TestFlight checklist); L4 `OUTLOOK_WEEK6_PERCENT_ENABLED` stays `false` — **lighting the flag is not lighting the percentage**. **The seam is designed rather than vaguely deferred:** beat `standing` is the recorded mount point for a playoff **band chip** under the existing invariant, so lighting later is additive and does not redesign the screen.
**Alternatives considered:** *Light `outlook.odds` and show bands only* — genuinely supported by the calibration (§8 allows bands from week 0) and still rejected: it buys #358/#359 nothing, breaks the feature on two live platforms, and spends the lighting decision on a surface that did not ask for it. *Source Sleeper's projections endpoint for the PPG claim* — rejected: undocumented, no commercial guarantee, deprecable without notice; if wanted it is a new external dependency on a gray-ToS endpoint needing its own scope and a `DEPENDENCIES.md` entry, not a smuggled-in UI detail. *Derive a team-level PPG proxy* — rejected, and this is the sharpest one: the proxy already exists as `RosterValueStrength`'s `mu = MEAN_POINTS + POINTS_PER_VALUE_SD·z(lineup_value)`, whose own source file calls it "a documented heuristic, NOT an empirically fit model" and whose three constants are measurably un-fit (`outlook_mean_points = 110` against a pooled empirical 130.6); rendering that as "+6 PPG" would be inventing a number *with a citation attached*, which is worse than inventing one plainly. *Honor championship odds in any form* — impossible: `title_pct` is unrenderable at any week by `docs/cross-client-invariants.md`, on an absence of skill (pooled +4.2 %, 90 % CI [−13.1 %, +20.0 %], 3 of 6 league-seasons worse than climatology, eight predictions above 0.4 containing one champion).
**Consequences:** #357 is answered by **`starter_impact.slots[].before/after`** with tier and positional rank (`trade.position_impact`, **already ON**) — "your TE slot goes TE31 → TE2, your RB slot RB3 → RB19" — which is *more* specific than a single PPG scalar and is in units the app already defends. **Retrospective team PPG rank stays in scope but degraded**: it is real measured data (`LeagueState.weekly_scores`), Sleeper-only, and empty until week 1, so the card names the actual reason (`preseason` / `platform_unsupported`) rather than hiding the row or gating the feature. The odds-free property is **mechanically pinned**, not merely intended: `test_no_odds_fields` asserts over the serialized JSON and `check-team-review.js` #3 asserts the screen imports no outlook client. `outlook.odds` remains `false` in `config/features.json` and NEXT.md item 7 is rewritten to state the debt in D-056 terms.
**Status:** **SUPERSEDED by [D-094](DECISIONS.md)** (operator override, same day). The *reasoning* below stands as the record of what the evidence said; the *conclusion* — keep the flag dark — was reversed by the operator. The forward-PPG cut and the championship-odds refusal in this entry are **NOT** superseded and remain in force.

## D-094 — Operator Lights `outlook.odds`, Reversing D-093; Bands Are Licensed, `title_pct` and Bare Percentages Are Not
**Date:** 2026-08-19 (operator override, verbatim: *"Outlook odds should be visible. Forward PPG cut. I waive maestro"*) — supersedes [D-093](DECISIONS.md), same session
**Context:** Hours earlier the same session recommended keeping `outlook.odds` dark ([D-093](DECISIONS.md)) on four grounds: Sleeper-only coverage, a preseason model at its weakest point (`completed_weeks == 0`, skill lower CI bound **+2.9 %**, 2 of 6 backtested league-seasons losing to climatology), an evidence debt written in Maestro terms that D-056 had already voided, and the fact that no feedback item actually required odds. The operator overruled it. **Two of those four grounds turned out to be weaker than the recommendation claimed, and that is worth recording rather than burying:** (1) the platform objection was *already solved in shipped code* — `LeagueSummaryScreen`'s `outlookSupported` selector gates the query on the league's platform, resolving unknown to supported, so ESPN/MFL leagues render **no section at all** rather than a 501 error; the recommendation cited an L2 criterion that was already met. (2) The preseason objection argued against *first impressions*, not against validity — the calibration explicitly supports **bands from week 0** (preseason skill excludes zero: +21.3 %, CI [+2.9, +39.1]) and withholds only the finer granularity. The genuinely load-bearing constraints were never about lighting at all; they are about *what renders once lit*, and they survive this reversal untouched.
**Decision:** `outlook.odds` → **`true`** in `config/features.json`, with a comment block stating precisely what the flip does and does not license. Lit: the built-but-dark #169 layer (`f27c0f5`) — the D-025 collapsed one-line "your outlook" strip, the full section behind it, and `GET /api/league/outlook`. **Not licensed, and unchanged:** (a) **`title_pct` stays unrenderable at any week in any form, banded or numeric** — an absence of demonstrated skill (pooled +4.2 %, 90 % CI [−13.1 %, +20.0 %]; 3 of 6 league-seasons worse than climatology; 8 predictions above 0.4 containing one champion), not a calibration judgement, and therefore not a thing an operator preference can license; (b) **`playoff_pct` renders only as the three-band chip** on the cross-client thresholds; (c) **`OUTLOOK_WEEK6_PERCENT_ENABLED` stays `false`** — lighting the flag is not lighting the bare percentage, which is a separate risk call on pooled, non-week-stratified calibration. Today's live posture is therefore: Sleeper leagues only, `meta.beta` true, **bands and row order, no win-loss numbers**, "Projected · preseason · beta" ribbon. **Evidence:** the operator waived the Maestro flow (already void under D-056); it is replaced by `mobile/tests/check-outlook-bands.js`, a new dependency-free structural guard pinning the two thresholds, the band→semantic-color map, the label-per-band rule, top-down bucketing, that no client reads `title_pct`, and that the week-6 percentage switch is off. **All six assertions were proven to fail under deliberate sabotage before the guard was accepted** (the 2026-08-10 lesson), and it gates CI automatically — `ci.yml`'s `mobile-typecheck` globs `tests/check-*.js`.
**Alternatives considered:** *Light it only from week 1 (D-093's L1)* — moot once the operator ruled, and the calibration supports bands at week 0 anyway. *Light the week-6 rounded percentage at the same time* — **not done**: the operator's words were "odds should be visible", which the band chip satisfies; a bare percentage is a distinct decision on weaker evidence and is left for an explicit call. *Read "outlook odds" as including championship odds* — **refused**: `title_pct` cannot be rendered on any authority, and the refusal is now mechanically enforced rather than merely documented. *Treat "I waive maestro" as waiving all evidence* — **rejected as an over-read**: Maestro was already retired by D-056, so the waiver clears a dead debt; D-056's replacement net (structural guard + code-walk + TestFlight) still applies, and the guard was written and sabotage-proven this session.
**Consequences:** The flip is **deploy-free on merge** (`POST /api/feature-flags/reload`) and needs **no client release** — the surface has shipped in every build since 2026-08-11. `NEXT.md` item 7 is closed. Team Review ([D-092](DECISIONS.md)) is re-planned to carry a playoff band chip on its `standing` beat, which was already designed as the seam for exactly this — so lighting cost the feature a payload field and a chip, not a redesign; its `test_no_odds_fields` guard is correspondingly narrowed from "no odds at all" to "no `title_pct`, no bare percentage". **The first TestFlight exposure is unverified at flip time:** no one has seen the lit surface on a device, and the operator's own league is the first read. **Two live risks are accepted rather than solved:** a preseason band can still be confidently wrong for an individual league (2 of 6 backtested league-seasons lose to climatology outright, one with an ordering correlation of +0.022), and IDP leagues price a minority of starting slots (7 of 15 in FFV3) so their bands are an offensive-core estimate — `meta.priced_slot_coverage` exists to caption this and **has never been rendered by any client**.
**Status:** Active. **Flag flipped on `claude/team-review-analysis-plan-1f91e3`, not merged and not deployed** — it goes live on the next merge to `main`.

## D-172 — Roster Fit Enters the Consensus Generator as a Sort-Key Blend, Not a Pool Term; Shipped Live at 0.5
**Date:** 2026-09-02 (PR [#261](https://github.com/mattmurf77/fantasy-trade-finder/pull/261) → `main` @ `c65a7998`; scope [docs/plans/consensus-fit-sort-key/scope.md](../docs/plans/consensus-fit-sort-key/scope.md), evidence [results.md](../docs/plans/consensus-fit-sort-key/results.md), code-walk [code-walk.md](../docs/plans/consensus-fit-sort-key/code-walk.md))
**Context:** A dual-agent review of the open TradesHome quality items (#350/#363/#393/#401/#414) converged on "the objective optimises board disagreement and roster fit is bolted on as a ±7.5% reorder" — correct for the divergence path, but that path is 15.5% of served cards. The lead's own read of the dispatch (`trade_service.py:6183`) and the consensus generator found the dominant mechanism on the other 84.5%: both sides are priced by the same public values, so the partner's gain is the exact negative of the user's, and the sign test `rv − gv ≥ ε` guarantees the viewer wins. Measured on the fixture harness (2026-09-01): partner-favourable share on the consensus path is **0 of N on every non-challenger arm in every cell**. Of the three reasons a counterparty could accept — values the players differently, wins on value, the trade fits their roster — the first is unavailable without a board, the second is forbidden by the sign test, and the third was not in the consensus sort at all. A pool-term prototype (`v3_pool_fit_extra`, worktree `agent-a9c4c705ebb53fc71` @ `60aa4572`, not merged) falsified "the pool grows, the deck doesn't change" — 15 of 17 divergence cards replaced at N=4 — but cost 5.5× at N=4 / 11× at N=6 (the review's 2.4× was N=2), unlocked junk-stuffed packages rather than the clean swap (the mirror 1-for-1 ranked 53,242nd of 137,558 behind 54,695 stuffed siblings), and left partner-favourable share flat.
**Decision:** Put fit into the **consensus generator's sort key**, as a blend, behind `consensus_fit_weight` (default 0.0 = byte-identical). `_generate_consensus_for_pair` selects candidates with two sorts, not a prune, and emits in pool order until the per-opponent cap — so the sort key decides which combos exist. At w>0 each pool sorts on `seed_value × (1 + w × fit_norm)`, `fit_norm ∈ [−1, 1]` = marginal-value-to-partner minus marginal-value-to-user on the shared consensus prices (both replacement levels from the rosters — no partner board exists on this path), negated on the receive side, normalised by the pool's max |fit|. The give side keeps `pos ∈ shed_positions` as its primary key. Picks are fit-neutral. Every gate is untouched; the user still wins on consensus on every card. Five sub-rulings:
- **Blend, never replace.** At w=1.0 a `fit_norm = −1` asset keys to 0 and sorts below every pick — a prune by the back door, the design just rejected. The live value is bounded by keeping the multiplier band strictly positive.
- **Live at 0.5, chosen by bound plus dose-response, not by the standard fixture.** 0.5 is the largest w with a strictly positive band (`[0.5, 1.5]`). The snake-drafted fixtures are a flat null at every w (no positional asymmetry to sort on — `need_fit_score` ≡ 0.5 there), so the number rests on the mirror fixture (deck moves from 0.25, holds at 0.5) and QA's 200-deck curve: emitted set changed 124/153/169/176 of 200 at w = 0.25/0.5/1.0/2.0, lead card 111/131/140/146, deck shrank 0 at every w — monotone and saturating, 0.5 captures ~87% of the reordering w=2.0 reaches. 0.25 is the only value in the corpus that shrank a deck cell.
- **Arm A pins the knob at the identity (0.0) rather than excluding it under the D-095 rule.** D-095's exclusion is only safe while the live row never moves; this knob ships expressly to be flipped. Unpinned, arm A would silently inherit the prod sort with no golden drift — and the existing arm-A golden is fit-insensitive, so it would not have caught it. `test_consensus_fit_pin_is_load_bearing` now proves the pin on a fixture that moves. Same shape as C4 `v3_shape_max_delta` (D-159). `MODEL_CHALLENGER_PROFILE` is deliberately unpinned: arm D is the live engine under an overlay and a partner-fit ranker is exactly what its PRD left open.
- **Seeded in `_MODEL_CONFIG_DEFAULTS` as well as `_DEFAULT_CFG`.** `set_config` raises on a row that does not exist, so the admin PUT can only reach a knob the boot migration has inserted. Deploy-then-flip, not flip-then-deploy.
- **Flipped on a Wednesday, censoring the bake-off window — accepted.** Prod `bakeoff_serve_interleaved` is 1.0 (G-064) and `bakeoff_include_challenger` 1.0, so arm D is serving and inherits the live row by design. Per D-099 a logged engine-affecting knob change mid-week censors the current window at its timestamp. The operator's instruction was "push it live once built"; the cost is one confounded arm-comparison week, not a user-facing effect, and rollback is one PUT. Do not read the 2026-09-01→09-07 bake-off window as a signal.
**Alternatives considered:** *The pool term as specified by the 2026-08-22 review (R7)* — prototyped, measured, rejected: cost and shape, above. *N=2 pool term alongside a junk-stuffing fix* — deferred, not rejected; it is the divergence-path sibling of this change and needs the surplus-inflation fix first. *Replace the sort key outright (w as a switch)* — rejected: a full-negative-fit asset would vanish from the pool. *Raise `need_fit_weight` instead* — rejected: `need_fit_score` is identically 0.5 on same-position swaps (give and receive terms sum to 1.0 by algebra) and saturated on the deep side of `_position_strength`, and it is a post-hoc reorder that cannot change which combos fill the cap. *Ship dark and flip Monday* — the D-099-clean option; declined by the operator's explicit instruction, recorded above.
**Consequences:** At 0.0 the deploy is byte-identical (goldens on the engine-quality and mirror fixtures captured from a `git archive origin/main` tree; QA's independent 37-scenario / 5,307-card fixture identical after stripping the probe field). At 0.5: card COUNT unchanged in 470 measured decks; the emitted SET is the same except where the gap sweetener's order-dependent `seen` dedupe substitutes one card for another (198/200 with the sweetener at 1539); on-screen order is by composite, which ties often (63/120 pairs), so pool order leaks through ties in ~5% of pairs. Cost is a fixed ~0.35 ms per pair (two `replacement_levels` calls, hoisted); the consensus emit loops carry no deadline, so a slower generator cannot shrink the deck. **What still is not modelled:** the counterparty *winning on value* — the consensus sign test stands. Arm D (both directions, 0.75 floor) is the built lever for that and is serving; its like/match readout split by basis is the next thing to read, once G-066 (arm C mis-stamps basis) is fixed so the split is trustworthy. Prod verification per results.md § After the flip: consensus impression counts split by basis before/after; for a viewer with `position_needs == []` and an unboarded partner thin where the viewer is deep, that partner's cards should now lead from the partner's deepest position.
**Status:** Active. Live at **0.5** since 2026-09-02 (`2026-09-02T18:04:26Z`; `model_config_changes` row source `admin-api`).

## D-171 — Find a Trade Pushes a Full-Screen Classic Deck; the Merged Landing Becomes the Builder Only

**Date:** 2026-08-31 (five verbatim operator rulings) · **Scope:** [`docs/plans/finder-results-push/scope.md`](../docs/plans/finder-results-push/scope.md) · **Amends:** the presentation halves of [D-158](#d-158--the-guided-tab-becomes-the-merged-in-league-page-the-canvas-anchor-is-a-deck-filter) and the #402 canvas-results rulings (D-169's decline cell goes flag-dormant with them) · **Closes by design:** gap-analysis C1/C4/C10 and the 2026-08-31 NEXT.md P0 ([2026-08-31 gap analysis](../docs/reviews/2026-08-31-find-a-trade-gap-analysis.md))

**The five rulings, verbatim decisions:**
1. **Landing = builder only.** The merged Trades landing keeps the inline trade-builder canvas, but the in-canvas results browsing (#402 `calc.canvas_results` behavior) turns OFF. Find a Trade is the only road to results.
2. **The D-153 fork is preserved.** Empty canvas ⇒ full model deck; give side ⇒ fairness-anchored `fair-packages` ideas. Both now land on a **pushed** full-screen deck page (`TradeDeck` + `resultsPush` param); the anchor receipt ("Built around X · Change") tops the pushed deck.
3. **"Edit in calculator" pops BACK to the landing with the trade pre-filled** (the MatchesScreen `canvasPrefill` route-param bridge; `popTo`, never navigate — G-056). Loop: build → results → tweak → results.
4. **✓/✕ on the pushed deck are exactly the classic deck's:** the swipe like + toast, the decline-reasons capture (overlay for calculator-origin), end-of-deck tally with "Back to calculator" (a plain pop — no prefill, the landing canvas still holds the build).
5. **Ships LIT for all users in the same build (v1.16.14).** The operator **explicitly overrode the dark-flag recommendation**; `calc.results_push` exists only as the kill switch, default TRUE.

**Mechanics:** the landing's Find a Trade runs the shared `forkCanvasSearch` (verdict + the one `calc_find_a_trade_tapped` row) then pushes the payload `{seq, opponent, origin, fairAnchor, anchorLabel}`; the pushed instance suppresses `canvasHost` by param presence, consumes the payload once into the calculator-arrival refs, and the ONE #330 choke point dispatches (no new mutate site). The landing under `resultsPushLive` retires the deck tree (`landingDeckRetired`), silences the choke point and the first-run auto-start, and forwards consumed store handoffs (league Offers) into the push. Restoring `TradeCard` as the results surface is the point: the likes-you "They're interested" pill (C1 — the D-170 loop's missing half), `trade_card_viewed` (C4) and the end-of-deck tally/See-liked (C10) all return with it.

**Alternatives considered:** re-homing the likes-you pill + analytics + tally onto the browse tiles (the gap analysis's literal P0 fix) — rejected by ruling: rebuilding deck affordances one at a time inside the canvas is the treadmill the push ends. Dark-flag rollout — recommended, explicitly overridden (ruling 5b). Reusing the store `FinderHandoff` for the push hand-off — rejected: the landing's own consumption effect would race the pushed instance for it; a route param is instance-addressed by construction.

**Consequences:** `calc.canvas_results` → **false** (four files + baked default; code intact, flag-dormant — full in-canvas restore = `results_push:false` + `canvas_results:true` + reload). First-run users get the builder, not an auto-started deck (accepted under ruling 1). #410's `browseDecline` cell and the #402 browse machinery are dormant, not deleted. Guards: new `check-results-push.js` (sabotage-proven ×5); `check-canvas-results.js`/`check-inline-home.js`/`check-calc-merged-behavior.js` re-pinned with dated notes.

**Status:** Active.

## D-170 — The ✓ Always Records the Like, and a Hand-Queued Like Always Surfaces (Removes D-152's Refusal Contract; Narrows D-096 to Non-Hand-Built Likes)

**Date:** 2026-08-31 (operator ruling, express lane — "push that live once done") · **Partially reverses:** [D-152](#d-152--the--cell-queues-a-hand-built-package-by-writing-the-decks-own-like-and-refuses-up-front) (the refusal half; the like-recording half stands) · **Narrows:** [D-096](#d-096--the-likes-you-injector-is-gated-the-floor-moves-into-the-units-the-user-reads-and-r1-runs-directionally-reverses-d-055-sub-decision-5--q-g6-1) (its gates now apply to engine/deck likes only) · **Built:** `POST /api/trades/queue` + `_inject_likes_you_cards_impl`, server-side only

**The ruling:** the ✓ queue button must ALWAYS record the user's like — the product-refusal validation gate is removed — and any liked trade must actually SURFACE in the opponent's deck even when it fails the fairness gate: "the fairness check was taking the decision out of the user's hands."

**Route side.** `_calc_queue_mirror_reason` (D-152's up-front, injector-mirroring predicate: roster actionability, the counterparty's untouchable/not-interested lists, the D-096 quality ladder) is **deleted**, along with the `likes_you_off`/demo-league early-outs and the owned-pick injection + FB-409 caller-member synthesis that existed only to feed it. Kept: the `calc.merged_layout` 404, `@_gate_unverified_write`, the structural 400s, the `calcq_` deterministic id + idempotency probe, and the swipe-identical record path. **One refusal survives, `not_league_member`**, for an `opponent_user_id` the session cannot resolve to a member (or the caller themselves): with no counterparty identity there is nothing to record a like *against* — that is structural unrecordability, not a product refusal. `CALC_QUEUE_REASONS` keeps all six values (cross-client enum, mobile `CalcQueueReason` union unchanged); five are now vestigial and never emitted.

**Injector side.** `load_recent_league_likes` now carries each row's `trade_id`, and `_inject_likes_you_cards_impl` exempts **hand-queued likes** (`trade_id` prefix `calcq_`) from the viewer's untouchable/not-interested/avoid-position skips and the entire D-096 ladder (floor + directional R1 + `filler_ok`) — the counterparty hand-built that exact package for this user, so it surfaces with the standard LIKES YOU treatment and an honest value bar. **Engine/deck likes (non-`calcq_`) keep every gate** — D-096 exists because mirrored likes were tilting against the receiving user, and this ruling narrows it only for explicit hand-built likes. Structural conditions still apply to both kinds: membership, roster actionability (a trade whose players left the rosters cannot render honestly), the `_LIKES_YOU_CAP`, past-swipe replay, and R4 dedup.

**Accepted costs, stated:** a hand-queued like can now put a package the viewer loses badly on — or one touching their untouchables — at deck position 1–3; the 2026-08-15 Phase A "insulting first card" finding can recur through this one lane, by operator choice. D-152's "a like the mirror would drop is worse than no like" argument is retired by making the mirror not drop it; the residual silent-drop window (roster drift between ✓ and the next deck) is accepted.

**Status:** Active.

## D-169 — The Merged Canvas Action Row's Middle Cell Becomes a Bare ✕ During a Browse Session; D-157's Misread Is Resolved by Making the Control Mean What Users Already Read It As

**Date:** 2026-08-30 (operator ruling, verbatim: *"It does mean pass / Keep the x button."*) · **Amends:** [D-157](DECISIONS.md) and `docs/feedback/items/402-more-offers-shop/canvas-results-spec.md` §4 · **Trigger:** feedback #410 · **Spec:** [docs/feedback/items/410-found-trade-decline-position/prd.md](../docs/feedback/items/410-found-trade-decline-position/prd.md)

**Context:** [D-157](DECISIONS.md) (2026-08-23) replaced a bare ✕ in this exact cell with the word "Clear" after tester Segrave (build 128) read the glyph as the deck's pass control and silently wiped the canvas he had just built. The #402 canvas-results operator session then wrote the placement into the spec as a contract clause: *"A ✕ control on the browsed idea (placement: with the pager, never inside the action row's 50/30/20 cells — that row's proportions are D-157 and unchanged)."* Feedback #410 asks for precisely the placement that clause forbids and precisely the glyph D-157 removed. The operator was shown both artifacts and ruled for the report.

**Decision:** During a live `calc.canvas_results` browse session with an idea fronted and the decline-reason machinery mounted, the merged calculator's action-row middle cell is the **decline control, rendered as a bare ✕** (`semantic.neg`, 16pt, `testID` `calc.action.decline`, `accessibilityLabel` "Pass on this trade idea"). In every other state — empty canvas, hand-built canvas, flag off, the pushed Real-values page, `FeaturedTradeWindow`, the #270 experiment, and under the `feedback.decline_reasons` kill switch — the cell remains the labeled **Clear** of D-157, byte-identical (`testID` `calc.action.clear`). The pager's ✕ is removed: exactly one decline control is ever mounted. The 50/30/20 proportions and the ✓ cell are untouched.

**Why this is not a straight reversal of D-157.** Segrave's misread was that the ✕ *meant pass when it actually cleared the canvas*. This decision makes the cell **mean pass** — it resolves the misread by making the control do what users already believed it did, rather than by teaching them it does something else. The operator's words are the ruling on that point: *"It does mean pass."* Two further facts made the ✕ safe here in a way it was not in D-157's world: the decline opens a **confirmable, dismissible two-layer reason overlay**, so a mistaken tap is backed out of rather than silently destructive; and the Clear it replaces was itself **actively corrupting browse state** — `clear()` emptied the canvas without ending the session and snapshotted `{[],[]}` into the browsed idea's edit map, so paging back restored a wiped idea instead of the engine's original (closed as PRD R-6 **for the action row**; QA-B F-1/F-2 recorded one residual flag combination — `calc.merged_layout` OFF with `calc.inline_home` + `calc.canvas_results` still ON leaves the stacked page's ghost `Clear trade` reachable and reproduces the corruption, and that state now has no decline control at all since the pager ✕ is gone. That is a kill-switch-only combination, not a shipped state; see PRD §6.6). D-157's *principle* — do not put an unlabeled control where users will misread it — is honored by the ✕ now carrying the meaning users assign it, and by the accessibility label carrying the verb the glyph does not.

**The narrow reading that preserves the spec clause's rationale.** canvas-results-spec §4's stated reason for keeping the ✕ off the action row was *"that row's proportions are D-157 and unchanged"*. This change does not touch them: it swaps the middle cell's **content**, not its **flex**. Only the placement sentence is overturned; its rationale survives intact and is re-pinned by the structural guard.

**Alternatives considered:** *A word — "Pass" / "Decline" — instead of the glyph* (the plan's own recommendation, on D-157's authority): **rejected by the operator, knowingly, after being shown D-157 and the spec clause.** *Leave the ✕ on the pager and merely disable the action-row Clear during browse*: removes the corruption but not the report — decline stays far from ✓ at a smaller visual weight. *Add a fourth cell (Find / Clear / ✕ / ✓)*: changes the D-157 proportions outright, and the operator's word was "replace", not "add". *Make the action-row Clear end the session during browse*: duplicates `handleBrowseClear`, which the receipt and pager already own.

**Consequences:** The same cell means "clear the canvas" in one state and "decline this idea" in another — accepted, mitigated by the glyph changing with the meaning and by the reason overlay's confirmability. `calc_cleared` stops firing during a browse session (a reduction in emission on one state, not a taxonomy change). D-157's tour beat **n19** was re-checked as D-157 requires: its copy (*"Clear became this cross. It records why you passed"*) now describes the UI more literally than before and is **unchanged**; its `target: 'trades.pass-btn'` is registered only by `TradeCard`, which canvas-results retires on this host, so the spotlight was already dark on this path before #410 — re-targeting it to `calc.action.decline` is Wave-B tour work, deliberately not done here. `docs/feedback/items/402-more-offers-shop/canvas-results-spec.md` §4's first bullet is amended in the same commit. **If a future tester misreads the ✕ the way Segrave misread it, this entry — not D-157 — is what gets revisited.**

**Status:** Active.

## D-168 — The Merged Canvas Partner Has Three States; Unscoped Is Boolean State + Null Id, Never a Sentinel; a Seed Prefill Never Counts as Chosen
**Date:** 2026-08-30 (FB-406 build, extending FB-407's chosen-partner convention; PRD `docs/feedback/items/406-target-any-leaguemate/prd.md`)
**Context:** Post-FB-407 the unscoped league-wide search existed only as the invisible default: the Team dropdown showed a manager the user never picked, and once `opponentChosenRef` flipped true there was no way back to unscoped short of a remount. Separately, FB-407's QA found that the #402 browse-session seeding effect initialized the ref `true` for a counterparty the user never chose, so Clear-after-browse silently ran a single-team sweep.
**Decision:** The merged canvas partner has exactly **three states** — **chosen** (scopes the search), **auto-default** (never scopes, labeled honestly by the `calc.search-scope-note`), and **explicit Anyone** (unscoped by choice, always reversible via the sheet's leading `calc.team-sheet.any` row). Unscoped is **boolean state (`partnerAny`) plus a null `opponentId` — never a sentinel id** in state comparisons or request bodies; the wire form of "any" is the *absence* of `opponent_user_id`. And a **browse-session seed prefill never counts as chosen** (R-10): the seeding write carries `seeded: true` and both chosen-ness initializers read `!!initialOpponentId && !seededPrefill` — in-session scoping rides the FB-407 receive-side clause instead, so an intact browsed idea still scopes while an emptied canvas honestly restarts league-wide, closing FB-407's known limitation. Render truth comes from `partnerChosen`, the ref's state mirror, written only on lines adjacent to the ref's two write sites with a textually identical initializer.
**Alternatives considered:** *Sentinel `'any'` partner id* — rejected: it leaks into evaluate keys/args and request bodies (guard A-8). *Promoting the ref itself to state* — rejected by the FB-406 critic (round 2, ruling 1): it rewrites FB-407's fresh guards for no behavioral gain. *Threading the Anyone label through the host so it survives a search* — rejected: host diff beyond R-10's one line, or a banned sentinel; the label is pre-search-only (NB-1).
**Consequences:** A chosen scope is now reversible; the honesty note renders iff the payload's `opponent` would be null (the predicate is the gate's exact complement, pinned whitespace-normalized). The evaluate derivation is partner-gated (`const ev = opponentId ? evalQ.data : undefined`) to close the `placeholderData` stale-verdict leak. Pinned by `mobile/tests/check-any-partner.js` (15 assertions, each sabotage-proven) and the re-specced `check-calc-merged-behavior.js` 20a.
**Status:** Active.

## D-167 — Age Preference Is a Consensus RE-PRICING With a Capped Boost, Not a Filter; User Boards Stay Untouched
**Date:** 2026-08-29 (operator decision; scope docs/plans/age-pref-value/scope.md)
**Context:** The trade-disposition review (docs/business/analytics/2026-08-29-trade-disposition-review.md) measured a monotone age preference on both sides of served cards: give-u23 like rate **9% (1/11)**, receive-30plus **14% (2/14)**, while the mirror shapes (receive youth 49%, ship 30+ 58%) are the deck's best performers — consistent across all three serving arms. The models price by value; testers also price by age. Operator ruled, mid-analysis: don't remove those cards — "add a multiplier to their values so it's harder to get them to appear / are treated higher value", "and there should be a maximum value increase as well."
**Decision:** A single **symmetric age multiplier on the CONSENSUS value accessors** used by deck generation (`trade_service._vs`, `trade_optimizer._sv`, `trade_gen_v2.cval` — all three arms priced identically): u23 × `age_pref_mult_u23` (1.10, increase capped at `age_pref_boost_cap` = 500 value points), 30plus × `age_pref_mult_30plus` (0.90), 23–29 / picks / no-age untouched. One mechanism yields all four measured preferences: giving u23 needs more back and receiving 30plus credits less (both shapes get rarer via the #108 consensus user-gain gate), while receiving youth and shipping age get easier. Sub-rulings: **(1) re-price, never filter** — no card shape is banned; **(2) user/opponent BOARDS are untouched** — a user's own rankings are their stated opinion and are not editorialized; the adjustment expresses the population prior on the consensus lens only; **(3) the cap bounds increases only** (any band, future-proof) and is band-agnostic; **(4) knobs, not a flag** — both mults at exactly 1.0 short-circuit to byte-identical accessors, so `PUT /api/admin/config/<key>` is the deploy-free kill; **(5) arm A pins both mults at 1.0** in `MODEL_A_PROFILE` (pre-wave engine had no age preference; golden un-recaptured), so the bake-off's baseline stays honest while current/challenger/gen_v2 all get the adjustment.
**Alternatives considered:** *Ordering-layer penalty (F3/F5-style multiplier in `_order_deck`)* — rejected: reorders but still spends deck slots on shapes users won't take, and the operator asked for value semantics. *Side-aware perceived-value multipliers (boost give-side u23 only, discount receive-side 30plus only)* — rejected: two directional mechanisms where one symmetric one produces the same four effects, and directional pricing would make the two sides of the same card disagree on an asset's value. *Global value-model change (seed recalibration)* — rejected: blast radius includes the pick ladder calibration and every non-deck surface; the deck is where the evidence is.
**Consequences:** Consensus-basis card display values shift visibly (u23 assets read higher, 30+ lower) — intended, it IS the message. Asset-ideas / fair-packages / calculator / likes-you surfaces are deliberately out of scope (own accessors); if the effect proves out on deck dispositions, extending them is a follow-up. The taste layer (F5) continues learning per-user age preference on top — this is the population prior, taste is the personal correction. Re-measure with the disposition review's query set once fresh serve data accumulates.
**Status:** Active.

## D-165 — #362 Ships LIT, #360 Stays Dark: a Promise Cannot Ship While a Third of the Deck Ignores It
**Date:** 2026-08-26 (operator ruling, at ship time for `feat/jon-360-362`)
**Context:** Both features were built dark on 2026-08-19 and held unmerged for a week. At ship time the operator asked for both flags lit. Checking that against production first surfaced a blocker that [Q-031](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) had predicted but recorded as hypothetical: **`bakeoff_serve_interleaved` reads `1.0` in live prod `model_config`, not the `0.0` the seed and Q-031 both assumed**, and `bakeoff_include_gen_v2` is `1.0`. The `gen_v2` arm therefore serves one of the three ten-card groups of every organic deck today. `backend/trade_gen_v2.py` contains **zero** references to `avoid_positions`, `acquire_positions` or `trade_away_positions` (it does honor `not_interested_ids` and `untouchable_ids`, six references each). Lighting `trade.avoid_positions` would therefore have shipped a hard exclusion that silently fails on roughly a third of served cards — and #360's PRD states the contract as *"a promise, not a preference … if you avoid QB, no card in your deck contains a QB coming to you. Full stop."*
**Decision:** **`trade.standing_offers` → `true`** (four files per [G-062](GOTCHAS.md); not added to `LAUNCHED_FLAG_DEFAULTS`, per [D-166](#d-166)). **`trade.avoid_positions` stays `false`**, upholding [Q-032](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) rather than reversing it. #362 is unaffected by the gen_v2 gap: it injects through the likes-you path, and both its prerequisites (`trade.likes_you`, `trade.picks_in_pool`) are already live. Verified before flipping that `standing_offer_inject_cap` seeds at `2.0`, not `0` — had it seeded at the kill-switch value the flip would have shipped nothing while reading as a launch.
**Alternatives considered:** *Light both and accept the leak* — rejected by the operator; the failure is invisible to the user and converts a stated promise into a lie about a third of the time. *Set `bakeoff_serve_interleaved` to 0 first* — would close the leak and also repair Chasing/Shopping, but pauses the bake-off currently being measured; not this release's call. *Port the three preference families into gen_v2 first* — the real fix, but new scoped work, and #362 should not wait behind it.
**Consequences:** #362 goes live to TestFlight on this merge with **no runtime evidence** — its checklist is unrun, which the operator accepted explicitly. `trade.avoid_positions` remains one deploy-free flip away once gen_v2 honors positional preferences. **A pre-existing production defect is now on the record rather than implied:** Chasing and Shopping have been silently ignored on the gen_v2 share of every real deck since whoever raised that knob above 0 — unrelated to this branch, and rewritten into Q-031 as live rather than hypothetical.
**Status:** Active.

## D-166 — A Kill-Switch or Dark Flag Is Never Listed in `LAUNCHED_FLAG_DEFAULTS`
**Date:** 2026-08-19 (branch `feat/jon-360-362` — feedback #360/#361/#362)
**Context:** Both new flags (as originally drafted: `trade.avoid_positions`, lit; `trade.standing_offers`, dark — both postures were later reversed, see [Q-032](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) and [D-165](#d-165)) carried a written instruction to appear in mobile's `LAUNCHED_FLAG_DEFAULTS` — #360's PRD hazard A-10 said so outright, and #362's SC-14 said the client default must "agree with `config/features.json`". The instinct has a real defect behind it: `revalidateFlags` does a whole-map `set({ flags })`, so a flag lit in config but absent from that map resolves falsy on frame one and true after the first fetch, and the surface visibly pops in. The same reasoning is already recorded in that file for the #300 pair. It is nevertheless the wrong trade here, and this session made the mistake once on `outlook.odds` before a parallel session caught it.
**Decision:** **That map fails OPEN by design** (its own `#115` comment: a first-ever boot with no cached map, or a failed revalidate, keeps listed features ON). Listing a flag therefore trades a one-frame cosmetic pop-in for a hole in the kill switch:
- **A lit kill-switch flag** (`trade.avoid_positions`) listed there keeps rendering its surface on a client whose revalidate failed **after the operator killed the flag** — the Trade DNA sheet would go on accepting an Avoiding set the engine has stopped honoring. A silently-ignored user promise is strictly worse than a pop-in.
- **A dark flag** (`trade.standing_offers`) listed there is worse: a first-ever boot **lights a dark feature**. #362's SC-14 as written would have pinned that bug as a requirement.
**The rule:** a flag belongs in `LAUNCHED_FLAG_DEFAULTS` only when it is a **graduated, launched** feature whose failure mode is "stays on" — never when it is a kill switch or a dark launch. Accept the one-frame paint-in. `mobile/src/api/league.ts:709` already carried this instruction for `outlook.odds` ("Never add `outlook.odds` to the launched-flag defaults"); this generalizes it beyond that one flag.
**Alternatives considered:** *List it and accept the kill-switch gap* — rejected; [D-094](#d-094) accepts two live calibration risks **specifically because** the kill is instant and total, so weakening the kill inverts a trade already made. *Make the map fail closed* — rejected as out of scope with a much larger blast radius: #115 exists because an empty first-boot flag map hid live ESPN linking.
**Consequences:** Both new flags are absent from the map; both features accept a one-frame pop-in. Two check assertions were inverted rather than written as specced (#362 SC-14b now asserts **absence**). The reasoning is written into comments at every gating site so it is not re-litigated. Recorded as PRD deviations in both item folders, because QA tests against the PRD. **Note for whoever audits this file:** the index table below is missing rows for D-095 and D-097 (concurrent-session drift, not introduced here) — worth a `living-memory-format-check` pass.
**Status:** Active.

## D-097 — The App May Not Call a Trade Balanced Below Its Own Bar; the Claim Is Removed, Not Replaced
**Date:** 2026-08-19 ([scope](../docs/plans/consensus-balance-claim/scope.md) — code-walk proof §7, TestFlight checklist §8; branch `fix/balanced-claim-fairness-gate`)
**Context:** The arm-B engine audit's *Bugs neither review found* table, row 2. `TradeCard.tsx` rendered *"This league-mate hasn't ranked players yet — this is a balanced trade by consensus value."* gated on `data.basis === 'consensus'` **alone**, with no fairness check of any kind; `web/js/app.js` carried the identical string as the `consensus-tag` tooltip, equally ungated. The app's own bar for balanced is **0.75** (`NORMAL_LOW` = `FAIRNESS_ON_THRESHOLD`), but the mobile fairness default flipped **OFF** on 2026-08-17, so the live generation floor is **0.50** and cards ship down to 0.501. Measured read-only on prod `deck_impressions`: **805 of 7,293 served consensus cards (11.04%)** carried that sentence while below the bar — band `[0.501, 0.75)`, p10 **0.7302**, p50 **0.8590**. Not a ranking-quality complaint: the product was asserting something untrue **by its own definition**. The audit had originally misattributed it to backend copy (`trade_narrative.py`), which **no client renders**; the defect is entirely client-side.
**Decision:** The consensus sub-line carries the balance claim **only at or above 0.75**. Below the bar — and whenever fairness is unknown — it **truncates to its true half and stops**: `This league-mate hasn't ranked players yet.` Two strings, character-identical on both clients, authored in exactly one place (the new pure module `mobile/src/utils/consensusNote.ts`, which `TradeCard.tsx` renders and which `mobile/tests/check-consensus-balance-claim.js` transpiles and RUNS). Four sub-rulings:
- **The claim is REMOVED, not replaced** (operator, 2026-08-19: *"We don't need to add the copy suggested.. We already have already features that provide the value summary/snap assessment on trade valuation"*). An earlier revision of this fix put honest replacement copy below the bar (*"priced from public values, not an even split"*); it was struck, correctly. The same card already renders `TradeValueBar` with `give_value`/`receive_value`/**`favors`**/**`gap`**, so direction *and* magnitude are on screen from the component its own comment calls the universal value verdict — prose about value would restate it. **This also settles directional wording** ("leans your way"/"leans theirs", the PRD's B2 line): duplication, and on web it would additionally require plumbing `give_value`/`receive_value` into `web/js/app.js`, which carries neither. **Do not re-open it.** The division of labour is: *the bar is the verdict, the sentence is the basis.*
- **The explanation half is never dropped.** *"This league-mate hasn't ranked players yet"* is the only thing on the card that says **why this is a fair-value idea rather than a divergence card**; nothing else conveys it. So the line truncates — it is never hidden. Hiding it would have traded a false statement for a missing one.
- **The fail-safe direction is DOWN, structurally.** Both clients compute `balanced` as a single conjunct (`typeof === 'number' && Number.isFinite(…) && … >= 0.75`), so an absent or non-finite score cannot mint the claim at all rather than merely being tested not to. `Number.isFinite`, **not** the coercing global `isFinite` — `isFinite('0.9')` is `true`, and a stringified payload would otherwise reach the comparison and compare as a string.
- **The bar is a CONSTANT, not a knob, and deliberately not the generation floor.** The floor moves (0.50 today, because of the 2026-08-17 toggle default); the *definition* of balanced must not move with it. Binding the copy to the floor would make the sentence self-fulfilling — whatever the app generated would be called balanced, which is the same failure one level up. `CONSENSUS_BALANCED_MIN` **re-exports** `NORMAL_LOW` rather than declaring a fourth literal; the four spellings (`NORMAL_LOW`, `FAIRNESS_ON_THRESHOLD`, `CONSENSUS_BALANCED_MIN`, web's `FAIRNESS_BALANCED_MIN`) are now pinned to each other by the structural check. **They already agreed at 0.75 — by coincidence of maintenance, not by construction.** Nothing had ever tested it.
- **It ships unflagged.** A flag's OFF state must be a safe fallback; here the OFF state *is* the defect. A switch whose only function is to turn the lie back on is not worth building or maintaining. Rollback is a revert.
**Alternatives considered:** *Keep honest replacement prose below the bar* — **rejected by the operator**, and the reasoning generalises: when a component already renders a value verdict, a sentence restating it is not extra honesty, it is duplication that will drift from the bar. *Hide the line entirely below 0.75* (the literal reading of the audit's "show the consensus line only when `fairness_score >= 0.75`") — rejected: the explanation half is true and is the card's only account of why it is a consensus idea; dropping it silently loses information the user has no other way to get. *Change the `Fair-value idea` label too* — rejected: the label names the **pricing basis**, true of every consensus card in every band; moving it would put the fix in two places and let label and body drift. *Make 0.75 a `model_config` key so the operator can tune it* — rejected: a tunable definition of "balanced" is not a definition. *Fix mobile only and follow up on web* — rejected outright, and the structural check now makes it un-shippable: a string fixed on one surface and stale on the other means the two clients **disagree about the same card**, which is worse than the original bug. *Add a backend field carrying "is balanced"* — rejected as unnecessary: both clients already had `fairness_score` in scope at the exact call site making the claim.
**Consequences:** For the **6,488 of 7,293 (88.96%)** prod cards at or above the bar, the rendered bytes are **unchanged**. About **1 in 9** consensus cards now shows a shorter sentence that makes no claim about balance, with `TradeValueBar` carrying the value story as it already did. Zero backend files touched, so no route, schema, migration, or engine line moves; two siblings working in `trade_service.py` / `bakeoff_profiles.py` / the likes-you injector are unaffected. `docs/cross-client-invariants.md` gains **§ Consensus balance claim**, which was pre-committed rather than discretionary: `docs/plans/trade-presentation-v2/scope.md:126` had recorded that `NORMAL_LOW` becomes an invariant "**if web or the extension ever renders this band**" — web now does. The cross-client guard is the most valuable artefact here and is built to survive rewording: it **reconstructs both web strings from web source and compares them byte for byte against the mobile module's output**, so it catches silent *drift* (sabotage S3b: web keeps its gate but says something different) and not merely a missing gate. **The adjacent defect is NOT fixed here:** `tradePresentation.counterpartyStatement()` (`:260-265`) asserts *"…is likely to be interested in this deal"* **unconditionally**, from a function its own docstring forbids from reading any fit or value field — structurally the same defect class, currently **dark** behind `trades.presentation_v2: false`. Recorded so it is not lost: **that surface must not be turned on with this string as-is.**
**Status:** Active. **Committed to `fix/balanced-claim-fairness-gate`, not pushed and not merged.**

## D-095 — The Landability Challenger Is a FOURTH Arm, Not a New Arm A; Both-Ways Ships With Its Floor
**Date:** 2026-08-19 (branch `feat/bakeoff-arm-a-challenger` — [PRD](../docs/plans/landability-challenger/PRD.md), [scope](../docs/plans/landability-challenger/scope.md); evidence [consolidated audit](../docs/reviews/2026-08-19-armb-audit-consolidated.md))
**Context:** A two-round, seven-agent audit of the live engine established that the finder is *a market-even engine that only shows the side where the viewer wins, then ranks that side by the biggest name*: **84.5%** of served cards never see a partner board, **96.3%** of 1-for-1s exist in only one direction, the user receives more than they give on **86.3%** of consensus cards and **86.9%** of boarded-pair cards, and `_tier_mult_v2` spans **4.57×** against a fairness span of 2.00× so an elite card outranks a perfectly even solid one at fairness 0.625 — while taking 60% more than it gives. That is a product choice (KTC and Dynasty Daddy print both sides of an even trade; we print the steal), not a bug. The operator's first instruction was *"I want these changes to be the new Arm A for the bakeoff"*.
**Decision:** Build the alternative as **arm D, `challenger`**, and leave arm A alone. The four levers ship as a thread-local config overlay (`bakeoff_profiles.MODEL_CHALLENGER_PROFILE`, entered by `model_challenger()`) on the live v1/v3 path: `user_elo_shrink` 0 (shrink neither board), `consensus_both_ways` 1 (drop the `rv ≥ gv` sign test, enumerate 1-for-2), `consensus_fairness_floor` 0.75, `need_gate_min_value` 0 (R5 off), and the tier ladder compressed to 1.15/1.08/1.00/0.90/0.80. Four sub-rulings:
- **Arm A is not touched, and the "new arm A" framing is rejected.** [D-075](#d-075--arm-a-is-a-pinned-constant-with-a-golden-not-a-set-of-knobs-someone-remembers) makes arm A a pinned constant with a golden precisely so the bake-off has one fixed point; replacing it makes every comparison unfalsifiable with no visible symptom. `MODEL_A_PROFILE`, `model_a()`, `MODEL_A_REFERENCE_SHA` and the golden's captured deck are byte-unchanged — the only edit to `test_bakeoff_arm_a_golden.py` is five new names in `_PINNED_KNOBS`.
- **The new knobs are EXCLUDED from `MODEL_A_PROFILE`, and the exclusion runs opposite to every other row on that page.** Every other exclusion is "a knob postdating the reference SHA, disabled so arm A behaves as it did." These three have the reverse shape: their **defaults are the pre-wave engine**, so pinning a kill value would make arm A skip shrinkage and emit both directions — behaviour it never had. Recorded in [scope-phase2.md](../docs/plans/three-model-bakeoff/scope-phase2.md).
- **Both-ways never ships without the floor.** The two knobs are separable in code and inseparable in intent: dropping the sign test at the live 0.50 client toggle is a 2:1 user-pays flood. At 0.75 the worst either side can be out is exactly `1 − 0.75` = 25%. Measured offline over the audited window: 0.75 both-ways yields **200.5%** of the one-way baseline's cards, **61.2%** user-pays.
- **No R4 bypass.** Arm A bypasses the windowless awaiting/matched exclusion because R4 postdates its reference SHA and has no kill knob; arm D has no such excuse. Re-serving a trade the user already has pending is not a more landable trade, and it would depress the arm's like rate for a reason unrelated to what is under test.
**Alternatives considered:** *Replace arm A, as first instructed* — built, then discarded when the PRD landed; it is the one change D-075 exists to prevent. *Convert R5 into a ranker rather than disabling it* — built (a `SoftNeedGate` carrying a composite penalty, with a partner-need relief term) and discarded: the PRD disables R5 by knob instead, and a partner-need ranker is a design question this profile does not try to settle. *Compress the tier ladder with a new `consensus_tier_mult_compress` knob* — discarded in favour of profile-only overrides of the five existing `tier_mult_*` keys, which needs no new `_DEFAULT_CFG` surface at all. *A symmetric ε > 0 on the consensus gate* — impossible, not merely rejected: both sides are priced through the same `seed_value` functional, so user and partner surplus are exact negatives. *Shrink both boards instead of neither* — `LeagueMember` carries no confidence map, so the partner's comparison counts do not exist to plumb; that is a schema change and a later PRD.
**Consequences:** **Dark.** `bakeoff_serve_interleaved` stays 0, so the arm generates, attributes (`deck_impressions.model_arm = 'challenger'`) and logs while users keep seeing arm `current`. Cost is one extra full `generate_trades` per organic bake-off job, sequential on the existing thread, killed by `bakeoff_include_challenger` = 0. **Arm B is byte-identical**, proved rather than asserted: `test_bakeoff_challenger.py` carries goldens captured at `origin/main` `50e0451` — code that had never heard of the knobs — for both a full `generate_trades` deck and the consensus generator. **The success metric is a known tension, ruled and closed:** the operator accepted like rate for a two-sided arm after hearing the objection that a two-sided challenger will underperform on a one-sided metric. Do not re-open it; the PRD's C2 mitigation is to split like rate by `basis` (84.5 / 15.5) rather than pool it. Analysis on 2026-08-16…08-19 stays contaminated by [D-091](#d-091--a-leagues-draft-pick-horizon-is-derived-from-league-state-and-enforced-at-the-writer). Naming is load-bearing: `model_arm` is a stored column, so `challenger` and `baseline` may never share a value.
**Status:** Active. **Committed to `feat/bakeoff-arm-a-challenger`, not pushed and not merged.** Track B (likes-you floor, "balanced" copy) is explicitly NOT in the arm — both are serving/client layers a profile overlay cannot reach, and both are owned elsewhere.

## D-098 — The Fit Challenger Is a Generator (Arm-C Pattern), Not a Config Profile
**Date:** 2026-08-20 (formal ADR: [ADR-013](../docs/adr/adr-013-fit-challenger-is-a-generator.md); PRD [fit-challenger](../docs/plans/fit-challenger/PRD.md) §3 knockouts operator-CLOSED)
**Context:** The fit challenger inverts live structure — value knockouts (consensus `rv ≥ gv`, dual-surplus floor, divergence prune, filler, #108) become dual 0–100 scores; preferences move to post-score filters; K1 widens past `|g−r| ≤ 1`. Those are code paths, not knob values: no `_cfg_override` profile (the arm-D pattern) can express them.
**Decision:** Standalone `backend/trade_gen_fit.py` invoked only via `bakeoff_runner.gen_fit_cards`; arm id `fit` OUTSIDE `ENGINE_ARMS` (no basis-split groups; `fairness_threshold = None` — its bar is the score stack). Live predicates called through the module namespace (`ts.overpay_ok(...)`) — never bound by name, never forked, so trade_service fixes propagate (the audit's import-binding no-op trap). All 17 `fit_*`/roster knobs carry arm-A disposition sentences.
**Alternatives considered:** a `MODEL_FIT_PROFILE` overlay (cannot reach the code paths); forking the predicates (drift; the audit's two-paths-disagree bug 3).
**Consequences:** Organic path proven byte-identical (`test_organic_never_imports_fit` + fixture generate); kill switches `bakeoff_include_fit = 0` / `bakeoff_serve_fit = 0`, both deploy-free; ~1.8 s/job at the 5,000 package cap on fixtures. Fixture dry run: 253 distinct legal ideas vs arm B's 12 on the same league (R-8 gate → operator decision before rostering).
**Status:** Active. Committed to `claude/trade-suggestions-review-69c9eb`, not pushed, not merged.

## D-099 — Bake-off Serving: Screen-Then-Confirm Rounds, Arm B Always Seated, Plain Draft, Censored Windows
**Date:** 2026-08-20 (formal ADR: [ADR-014](../docs/adr/adr-014-bakeoff-serving-rounds.md); reasoning record = dual adversarial drafts + cross-critiques in [docs/plans/fit-challenger/](../docs/plans/fit-challenger/))
**Context:** Every arm but `current` has generated dark and produced zero user decisions ever; the one serving attempt (2026-08-18) died to lane-quota deck shrink; tester supply is ~400 decided cards/week, so k served arms divide a fixed budget (10pp read at k=4 ≈ 4–5 weeks — past the window this repo's change velocity historically contaminates).
**Decision:** (1) `bakeoff_group_size = 0` is the serving posture — plain per-arm team draft, no quotas to under-fill; a zero-card arm costs nothing (regression-tested). (2) Screening windows may serve 3 arms at 15pp/non-inferiority resolution; a 10pp verdict requires a k=2 confirmatory round vs arm B; promote/iterate/kill rules pre-registered; pooled like-rate is guardrail, never verdict. (3) A logged mid-window knob change CENSORS the window at its timestamp (split, keep both segments); whole-window discard only for re-ranker contamination or unlogged changes. Control arm frozen during rounds; roster/serve-bit moves land on Monday boundaries and count as the week's one engine-affecting change.
**Alternatives considered:** hard max-2-arms always (rejected — silently re-ranks the operator's variety goal where power doesn't bind); serve all 4 built arms at once (rejected — no 10pp verdict inside a clean window); whole-window discard on any knob change (rejected — wastes ~25% of a round's evidence for a timestamped flip).
**Consequences:** `gen_v2` re-enters serving only at ≥2 leagues with 3+ boards (league-captive sample otherwise); fit's round is gated on a 3-day dark soak behind `bakeoff_serve_fit`; the Friday readout diffs every run's `config_json` against round start; the 3-week mark is a review trigger, not a ceiling.
**Status:** Active. Serving knobs unchanged this session (`bakeoff_serve_interleaved` still 0) — flips are the operator's, per the [PRD-build decision register](../docs/plans/fit-challenger/PRD-build.md).
## D-100 — The Sell List Is Where the MARKET Is Higher Than You; the Direction Is Fixed Upstream, Not Papered Over in Team Review
**Date:** 2026-08-20 ([scope](../docs/feedback/items/364-team-review-fixes/scope.md), [code-walk](../docs/feedback/items/364-team-review-fixes/code-walk.md) §1; branch `claude/team-outlook-experience-27a7a1`)
**Context:** Feedback #367 (`mattmurf77`, v1.15.0, screen `TeamReview`): *"Think the easiest sells and buys page is backwards. I should sell guys I'm lowest on on my roster and buy guys I'm highest on above the market not on my team."* Confirmed in source, and it was two independent errors, not one. (1) `trends_service.compute_consensus_gap` selected sells as `gap = user_elo − community_elo > 0` under a comment reading *"Only surface players where YOU value them ABOVE the market"* — the set the league will **not** pay up for. (2) `team_review._divergence` then assigned `easiest_sells → higher_than_market` and `easiest_buys → lower_than_market`, crossing both literal field names, so the user's best BUYS rendered on screen under **"Skip these — you'd be buying at a price you don't believe."** `easiest_buys` itself was always correct: it compares against the **owner's** elo, so `user − owner > 0` means the owner sells cheap. The two source ladders (league-community, consensus-seed fallback) also disagreed with each other about what the same field name meant.
**Decision:** Fix the **selection upstream** in `compute_consensus_gap` (`gap = community_elo − user_elo > 0`), which repairs the mobile Trends screen at the same time, and un-cross the field mapping in `team_review._divergence` on **both** ladders. Four sub-rulings:
- **`gap` is a POSITIVE edge magnitude on both sides.** Chosen over keeping a signed `user − comparison` so that every renderer already printing `+gap` (`TrendsScreen.tsx:443`, `web/js/app.js:6239`, the Team Review beat) needed no change, and so the two ladders could share one convention. `rank_gap` flipped with it (`_rank_delta(u_rank, c_rank)`) so the rank view and the elo view point the same way. **A client must never infer direction from the sign** — pinned in `docs/cross-client-invariants.md` § Consensus-gap direction.
- **Upstream, not contained** (operator's call when the blast radius was surfaced: *"Fix upstream — repairs Trends too"*). Trends was showing the same inverted list, and its label — *"Easiest sells (you rank them higher than league consensus)"* — described the old filter **accurately**, so it had to move with the backend or the two surfaces would disagree about the same players.
- **The field names were kept, and made true.** `higher_than_market` / `lower_than_market` are literal, so the fix is to put the right list in each rather than rename to `sell_candidates` / `buy_candidates`. Renaming would have 404'd the shipped build 122+ client on `d.higher_than_market.length` — an undefined-read crash — for zero semantic gain.
- **The asymmetric comparison is deliberate and stays.** Sells compare against the **community mean**, buys against the **specific owner**. You sell into a market; you buy from one person. That asymmetry is exactly why the buys side was right and the sells side was not.
**Alternatives considered:** *Fix only inside Team Review* — rejected by the operator; it would leave Trends inverted and the two clients contradicting each other. *Keep the signed `u − c` convention and only flip the filter* — rejected: it makes every sell row render a negative number under a `+` sign and leaves the two ladders on different conventions. *Rename the payload fields* — rejected, crashes shipped clients. *Treat "easiest" as ease-of-transaction rather than profit* — considered and rejected on the economics: a player you rate above the market is both unprofitable **and** hard to move, so both readings agree the shipped filter was wrong.
**Consequences:** Mobile Trends, web Trends and the Team Review divergence beat all change content for real users; the Trends sells label was rewritten to match. `web/js/app.js` copy was **deliberately left untouched** — "Easiest sells from your roster" and "No over-valued players on your roster" stay true under the corrected direction. **No flag reverts this**: `compute_consensus_gap` is ungated and shared, so rollback is a code revert, not a `features.json` flip — the one item on the TestFlight checklist to scrutinise hardest. One pre-existing test asserted the defect directly and was re-encoded (`test_consensus_gap_sells_are_where_the_market_is_higher_than_you`, now holding **both** roster players so it proves a selection, not just a sign); a second (`test_divergence_ignores_unjudged_players`) went **vacuous** under the fix — both lists came back empty while still passing green — and was repaired with a non-emptiness assertion. Sabotage-proven red, 3 of the 5 guards in this batch.
**Status:** Active. Committed on `claude/team-outlook-experience-27a7a1`; TestFlight pass owed.

## D-101 — Ship the Inference Model, Not Just Its Verdict: `window.model` Carries Every Knob `infer_team_outlook` Reads
**Date:** 2026-08-20 (operator instruction, verbatim: *"Be sure to build those values into the page itself so the user sees all of the inputs we use to determine team outlook"*)
**Context:** Feedback #365 reported the window beat calling an all-in team a rebuilder. While tracing it, the screen was found rendering **"Value age 23 and under"** against a `youth_age` knob of **26** (`trade_service.py:43`) — the threshold the user read was never the threshold the inference applied. `infer_team_outlook` reads seven knobs and returned only three shares plus a score, so the client had no honest way to describe its own inputs and had hardcoded prose instead.
**Decision:** `infer_team_outlook` returns a `model` block — `vet_age`, `youth_age`, the three `infer_w_*` weights, both `infer_*_cut` values — and `team_review._window` passes it through as `window.model`. The beat renders the full arithmetic: each share × its weight, the signed contribution, the total, and the two cuts the total is bucketed against, with the age labels read from the payload. Additive: existing callers reading only the share keys are untouched, and the block is **optional in the TS type** so an older payload hides the breakdown rather than rendering `undefined`.
**Alternatives considered:** *Import the knob accessor into `team_review.py`* — rejected: that module is a pure composer over service outputs and reaching into `trade_service._c` would break the property that makes it testable. *Pass the knobs from the route* — rejected as a third place for the same facts to drift. *Just correct the hardcoded "23" to "26"* — rejected: it fixes today's instance of a class of defect and leaves the next knob change to silently lie again. *Round the contributions to whole numbers* — rejected: the weights are order-1 and the cuts are ±0.08, so rounding erases the decision.
**Consequences:** Generalises the rule `equal_pick_share` already followed — **a client reads an encoding, it never restates one** — to the whole inference. It is also the honest partial answer to #365, which is otherwise unbuilt: the card now states in plain words that the model reads roster age and pick capital only, *not* record, lineup, or picks already traded away, so a young all-in team reading as "rebuilder" is explained on screen rather than mysterious. Adds a second consumer to seven `_DEFAULT_CFG` knobs, so **retuning any of them now changes user-visible copy** — intended, and the reason the values are not duplicated anywhere. Does **not** change the score, the buckets, or any deck: `infer_team_outlook`'s return value is otherwise byte-identical, which matters because the trade engine (`trade_gen_v2.py:986`, `trade_service.py:4250`) and the mock draft (`server.py:14013`) consume it. Changing the score itself is #365 proper and is specced, unbuilt, in [`plan-remaining.md`](../docs/feedback/items/364-team-review-fixes/plan-remaining.md) §1.
**Status:** Active.

## D-110 — Net First-Round Capital Enters the Score as a Weighted Term, Behind a Flag AND a Ledger, and Refuses to Score What It Cannot See
**Date:** 2026-08-20 ([scope](../docs/feedback/items/365-window-signals/scope.md), [code-walk](../docs/feedback/items/365-window-signals/code-walk.md); branch `worktree-agent-a3ea3b1d38e084930`, base `bc43b6f`)
**Context:** Feedback #365 (`mattmurf77`, v1.15.0, screen `TeamReview`): *"Window needs to do more than just age evaluation. I am all in for ffv3 league but it's identifying me as a rebuilder. We should look at signals such as number of 1sts owned vs traded away as one such signal."* The diagnosis was already settled in [`plan-remaining.md`](../docs/feedback/items/364-team-review-fixes/plan-remaining.md) §1: `infer_team_outlook` scores only `vet_share`, `youth_share` and `pick_share`, and `vet_age = 27` / `youth_age = 26` are adjacent, so every aged player is one or the other and a young all-in roster is misread **structurally**, not marginally. The blocker was never the signal — `draft_picks.original_user_id` and `.owner_user_id` have both shipped since #158 — it was the blast radius: `infer_team_outlook` is not a Team Review function. Its verdict feeds `outlook_alpha` for the trade engine (`trade_gen_v2.py:986`, `trade_service.py:4381`), the mock draft (`server.py:14013`) and the outlook seed (`server.py:5320`).
**Decision:** Add the term to the score — `score −= infer_w_net_firsts · clamp((acquired − traded_away) / own_total, ±infer_net_firsts_cap)` — behind **two** gates in series, and refuse to score an unverifiable zero. Four sub-rulings:
- **The flag AND a ledger, not the flag alone.** `trade.outlook_net_firsts` (default OFF) is the first gate; the second is that a caller actually supplies `first_round_ledger`, and **only `GET /api/league/team-review` does**. The engine, the mock draft and the outlook seed were deliberately left passing four positional arguments. So flag OFF is byte-identical for every caller (INV-365, proved against goldens captured by running the fixtures against the `bc43b6f` tree — code that had never heard of the kwarg — rather than against a re-derivation of the same formula, which would prove nothing), and **flag ON still moves no deck** (INV-365b). Deck movement now requires a second, deliberate change. Chosen over a single flag gate because a single gate makes "light it and see" an engine-wide experiment.
- **A weighted term, not a `not_sure` tie-breaker.** The operator asked for a *signal*; a tie-breaker that fires only inside the middle band is not one. The tie-breaker form was the cheaper option and was rejected on that ground, with the flag carrying the safety instead of the design.
- **`provenance` — the term refuses to score what it cannot see.** A league synced before pick provenance was captured has `original_user_id == owner_user_id` on every row and is **indistinguishable** from a league where nobody has traded a first. So one league-wide fact (`league_any_traded`) gates the term, and `signals.firsts.provenance` is `observed` / `none_traded` / `absent` with the card stating which. `applied` ships alongside because it is recoverable from neither `provenance` (the weight may be 0) nor `net_share == 0` (a genuine net of zero is a real, scored signal). Operator ruling, verbatim: degrade honestly and say so on the card.
- **The two new knobs ride `window.model`, inside the flag branch.** D-101's rule generalised: a term the score applies is a term the card renders. Adding `w_net_firsts` unconditionally would advertise a weight for a row that does not exist — the same defect class D-101 exists to stop.
**Alternatives considered:** *Move `infer_contender_cut` / `infer_rebuilder_cut`* — rejected; nothing in the real corpus argues they must move, and moving them changes the flag-off world. *Use raw `net` rather than `net / own_total`* — rejected: a 4-season horizon and a 2-season horizon would then produce different-magnitude terms for the same behaviour. *Read `is_traded` instead of comparing owners* — rejected: `is_traded` can be set on a row whose `original_user_id` is NULL, which tells you a trade happened but not whose first it was, and attributing it anyway would manufacture the signal. *Give league-mates the roster-only model while the caller gets the enriched one* — rejected: the `partners` beat pits your window against theirs, and two definitions of "contender" in one payload is the failure this whole build is arranged to avoid; every member gets the ledger. *Plumb the ledger into the engine now* — rejected as the change that actually moves decks; it needs its own scope, evidence and TestFlight pass.
**Consequences:** **Dark, and graduation is blocked on prod data — this is the most important line in the entry.** ~~On the only pick corpus reachable at build time (`data/trade_finder.db`) the operator has traded away zero of his own firsts and acquired one in both leagues that carry round-1 provenance, so the term as literally requested points him further toward rebuilder~~ — **SUPERSEDED SAME DAY. That was an artefact of an incomplete local corpus, and it was backwards.** FFV3 has no `draft_picks` rows locally, so the build-time reasoning drew on the two leagues that happened to be present and excluded the one that filed the report. The read-only prod query (276 round-1 rows, 7 leagues with a traded first) shows `mattmurf77` in **Fantasy Football Version 3 holding ZERO firsts having sold all three of his own — **3 sold, 0 acquired, 0 still owned** — stated as counts on purpose, because the two conventions in play have opposite signs: this prose had been reading "net +3" in a sold-minus-bought sense while the CODE scores `firsts.net_share` as owned-minus-traded, i.e. **−3**. Same fact, opposite sign, and neither had stated its convention. The scoring direction is correct either way (the score subtracts `w × net_share`, so a seller gains), the strongest all-in reading the term can produce, pointing him toward CONTENDER.** His other leagues run the other way (La Resistance −5, Lakeview −1), which is exactly the per-league discrimination the age heuristic cannot see. So the signal fires correctly on the very case that motivated #365, not contender; and **FFV3, the league in the report, has no `draft_picks` rows there at all** (and only a 291-byte `roster_data` stub, so the score cannot be recomputed locally either). Prod Postgres would settle both and was not reachable from the session. The term is therefore built, bounded and tested, and the operator must verify on prod before lighting it — see [scope §7.1](../docs/feedback/items/365-window-signals/scope.md). **Bound:** `infer_w_net_firsts = 0.10` against a `not_sure` band of ±0.08, and `|net_share| ≤ 0.75` across the 24 member-league pairs observed, so the contribution range is ±0.075 — the term can move an extreme team one bucket and can never move any team two. Two deploy-free levers: the flag, and `infer_w_net_firsts = 0` (which keeps the card showing the ledger while it stops scoring it). Evidence: 40 tests in `backend/tests/test_window_signals.py` plus `mobile/tests/check-window-signals.js` (10 claims); 20 sabotages, all red then green. One of those sabotages (S15) found a **vacuous assertion in the new client guard itself** — it matched an identifier that is destructured unconditionally rather than the rendered row — which is why the sabotage table requires the *named* guard to fail, not merely a red run.
**Status:** Active. **Committed to `worktree-agent-a3ea3b1d38e084930`, not pushed and not merged.** Do not graduate the flag without the TestFlight checklist §B.

## D-111 — The Playoff Band INFORMS the Window; It Never Replaces the Heuristic, and It Is Refused Outright in Preseason
**Date:** 2026-08-20 ([scope](../docs/feedback/items/365-window-signals/scope.md) §7.2, [code-walk](../docs/feedback/items/365-window-signals/code-walk.md) §3)
**Context:** Feedback #371 (`mattmurf77`): *"Coming back to the outlook bug, we should primarily use the playoff outlook value as the decision maker."* Reads as: stop inferring the window from roster shape and take it from the simulated playoff odds, which are a genuine model rather than a heuristic. Two facts make the literal reading unshippable. The odds engine is **Sleeper-only** — `backend/outlook/league_state.py` registers ESPN, MFL and Fleaflicker as `NotImplemented` stubs — while `infer_team_outlook` works anywhere a roster exists, so a straight replacement strands every non-Sleeper league with no window at all. And `completed_weeks == 0` is the simulator's weakest window (D-094: preseason skill lower CI bound +2.9 %, a 95 % preseason call realizing 78 %), which is precisely when a manager is setting his window.
