**Decision:** Behind `trades.window_from_odds` (default OFF), the band may **drive** `window.inferred` — `likely → contender`, `tossup → not_sure`, `unlikely → rebuilder` — but only when the flag is on **and** `outlook.odds` is on **and** the league is Sleeper **and** the simulator produced a row **and** `completed_weeks > 0`. Otherwise the roster heuristic drives. Four sub-rulings:
- **Both verdicts ship, always.** `window.source` says which model spoke and `window.roster_inferred` carries the heuristic's own answer **even when the band overrode it**. That is the difference between "informs" and "replaces", written into the contract instead of into a comment — and it is what lets the card say *"your odds read unlikely, so that is the call; roster shape alone said contender"* rather than silently swapping one number for another.
- **Preseason is a refusal, not an absence.** The band is still returned, with `odds_reason: "preseason"`, so the card shows what was available and says why it declined. Hiding it would leave the user unable to tell "we have no odds" from "we have odds and distrust them".
- **The ruling is a function, not an `if` in a route.** `team_review.resolve_window_from_odds` — because a rule that lives only inside a Flask handler is a rule nothing can test, and this rule is the whole of the operator's decision.
- **The map is a cross-client encoding.** `WINDOW_FROM_BAND` lives server-side; the client renders `odds.implied` and never re-derives it, and an unmapped band surfaces as `null` with a fallback rather than a silent `not_sure`.
**Alternatives considered:** *Replace the heuristic outright, as the report literally asks* — rejected on the platform stub and the preseason CI bound, both surfaced above; the operator pre-ruled this. *Blend the two into one score* — rejected: they are different units (a probability and a value-share score) with no principled exchange rate, and a blend hides which one moved. *Let the odds drive in preseason but mark the payload `beta`* — rejected: `meta.beta` already exists for the standing band and means "shown with less precision", not "trusted enough to set your trade preferences", which is what this beat's action writes. *Keep the odds view purely informational and never let it drive* — rejected as not answering the report at all. *Apply the odds window to the caller only, leaving `partners` on the heuristic* — rejected for the same reason D-110 gives league-mates the ledger: one payload, one definition of "contender".
**Consequences:** Changes **no** engine value — `infer_team_outlook` still runs, unmodified, and its verdict is what ships as `roster_inferred`. The odds-driven window does reorient the `partners` beat, which is intended and pinned. **Practically dark twice over:** the flag is off, and today `completed_weeks == 0`, so even lit it would refuse and fall back — the in-season half of the TestFlight checklist (§C4–C6) cannot be walked until week 1 is played, and that is stated rather than glossed. Independent of `trade.outlook_net_firsts`; all four flag combinations are valid and the checklist walks them. Evidence shares `test_window_signals.py` and `check-window-signals.js` with D-110; sabotages S10–S13 cover the refusal ladder and the payload shape.
**Status:** Active. Committed on `worktree-agent-a3ea3b1d38e084930`, not pushed and not merged.
## D-120 — Tier Bands Leave Value Space for Rank Space: "Elite" Is a Position-Relative Fact, Behind a Default-OFF Flag
**Date:** 2026-08-20 ([scope](../docs/feedback/items/366-tier-ladder/scope.md); branch `worktree-agent-a4ab94c51456abb78`)
**Context:** Feedback #366 (`mattmurf77`, screen `TeamReview`): *"Need one other layer for the startable bodies: Elite, Starter, Replacement… And would like to review the logic for tagging a player as 'elite'."* `_bin_player` cut on three absolute thresholds over `dynasty_value` — elite ≥ 4000, starter ≥ 1500, bench ≥ 500. But `dynasty_value(p) = ktc_max·e^(−ktc_k·(search_rank−1))` is a **monotone function of Sleeper's OVERALL `search_rank`**, so those three numbers were a disguised statement: "overall rank ≤ 73 / ≤ 151 / ≤ 238". Measured against the live pool (`data/trade_finder.db`, 2 684 players) that admits **33 elite RBs, 33 elite WRs, 17 elite QBs and 7 elite TEs**. One word, four meanings — exactly the report. The third bin (`bench`) had also been computed since the function was written and rendered nowhere; the beat printed `N elite · N starter` and dropped it.
**Decision:** Band by **rank within the position**, not by absolute value, behind `trade.position_tiers` (**default OFF**). Cuts derive from what a 12-team league starts (1 QB, 2 RB, 2 WR, 1 TE; superflex 2 QB): Elite = top **half** of the league's starting demand, Starter = inside **1.5×**, Replacement = inside **2.5×**. Four sub-rulings:
- **`bench` is NOT renamed.** `replacement` ships **alongside** it as an alias with an identical count. A rename would have broken every client older than this commit — including the shipped TestFlight build, whose `Depth` reads `tier_depth[pos]` directly — for zero semantic gain. Clients read `replacement ?? bench`; the user-facing label is "Replacement" always and the word "bench" is never shown. Pinned in `docs/cross-client-invariants.md` § Roster-profile depth bins and by `mobile/tests/check-team-review-depth.js`.
- **Per-position *value* thresholds were built and rejected.** O(1) and simpler, but the cuts must be derived once from a pool snapshot and hardcoded — and the snapshot lies. Deriving them from the live DB put **Tom Brady at QB18 and Derek Carr at QB32**: retired players carrying stale `search_rank`s, which would have been baked in permanently. Rank cuts self-calibrate.
- **The plan doc's stated mechanism is wrong, and it matters.** [`plan-remaining.md`](../docs/feedback/items/364-team-review-fixes/plan-remaining.md) §2 blames *"board-wide inflation silently promotes players"*. `search_rank` is an ordinal and cannot inflate. The real drift vector is a `model_config` retune of `ktc_k`/`ktc_max` — which **#117 already performed once** (`docs/runbook.md:441`: *"`_TIER_ELITE`/`_TIER_STARTER` value bins … now bind at market-sane depths"*). Same conclusion, different cause: the fix has to leave value space, not merely subdivide it.
- **The small-pool fallback is reported, not silent.** Positional rank is meaningless over a fixture. Below `_POS_TIER_MIN_POOL` (40) ranked players at a position, that position falls back to the absolute cuts, and the profile carries `tier_basis: {pos: "position_relative"|"absolute"}` so nothing has to infer which path ran.
**Alternatives considered:** *Positional rank from `trends_service._pos_rank_map`* — rejected: that ranks on **Elo**, a different value system, and `docs/feedback/items/300-league-rankings-trade-candidates/plan.md:223` already killed mixing the two for a user-facing count. *A `model_config` knob for the cuts* — rejected on the `GENERIC_PICK_SEEDS` precedent: a knob that silently re-bins every roster in production is worse than a revert, and the revert here is already a deploy-free flag flip. *Adding IDP positions while in the neighbourhood* — impossible, not merely out of scope: `database._SYNC_POSITIONS` is `{QB,RB,WR,TE}`, so defensive players are never ingested. The IDP blind spot `plan-remaining.md` attributes to `analyze_roster_strengths` is really at ingestion.
**Consequences:** **This is not a display flag.** `analyze_roster_strengths` also produces `position_needs` / `position_surplus`, consumed by `trade_gen_v2` (`:930`, `:980`) and `trade_service` (`:3413`, `:3440`, `:4096`, `:4172`, `:4259`) — ON **changes every deck for every user**. OFF, the function returns a dict byte-identical to pre-#366, pinned by `backend/tests/test_position_tiers.py` and sabotage-proven. Rollback is deploy-free (`config/features.json` + `POST /api/feature-flags/reload`). **Not graduated here.** A known graduation blocker was measured while building: the pre-existing engine suite (`test_roster_profile`, `test_need_fit`, `test_finder_targeting`, `test_presentment_rules` — 65 tests) is **completely insensitive** to this change, passing identically with the flag forced on, because every fixture pool is smaller than the guard and cannot distinguish the bands. Graduation therefore needs a deck-quality read on real leagues (`scripts/deck_eval.py`), not a green suite. Cost of the rank map is 1.31 ms over the real pool, memoized on the pool's identity (strong-ref, 2 slots — `id()` is unique only among live objects).
**Status:** Active, **dark**. Committed on `worktree-agent-a4ab94c51456abb78`; not pushed, not merged, flag not lit.

## D-121 — Handcuff Is Built on the Real NFL Depth Chart, Which FTF Already Ingests; the Plan Doc's "No Such Feed" Is Wrong
**Date:** 2026-08-20 ([scope](../docs/feedback/items/366-tier-ladder/scope.md) §0)
**Context:** Feedback #366's second ask: *"For just RB we also should have 'Handcuff' which should simply be the RB2 on every team."* [`plan-remaining.md`](../docs/feedback/items/364-team-review-fixes/plan-remaining.md) §2 assessed this as *"Handcuff needs a source FTF does not have… No current feed carries it"*, offered ingesting a depth chart (new dependency, new staleness surface) or approximating as *"second-highest-valued RB on the same NFL team"*, and recommended **not** approximating because that is wrong in exactly the committee backfields where the label matters. The recommendation was right; the premise was not.
**Decision:** Build it on Sleeper's own depth chart — `position == "RB"` ∧ `depth_chart_position == "RB"` ∧ `depth_chart_order == 2` — behind `trade.rb_handcuff` (**default OFF**). The feed was never missing; this is simply its first reader. Verified end to end before writing a line: schema `backend/database.py:970-971`, written on every sync `:8726` / `:8769-8770`, re-synced whenever `last_synced` is older than 24 h `:8652`, carried on the model `ranking_service.py:262-263`, hydrated onto every pooled `Player` `server.py:1580-1581`, and that pool is the `players` dict `analyze_roster_strengths` receives (`server.py:22815`, `trade_service.py:3169`). Live check: 149 of 603 RB rows carry a real `depth_chart_order`, and they are the 32 actual NFL charts (ARI Conner 1 / Benson 2 / Allgeier 3; BAL Henry 1 / Hill 2; …). The 454 nulls are camp bodies and free agents — correctly nobody's handcuff. Three sub-rulings:
- **It is an OVERLAY, not a fourth tier.** An RB2 is *also* Elite, Starter or Replacement. It is therefore a separate top-level `handcuff_rb` count and is deliberately **not** a key inside `tier_depth[pos]`, whose contract is a disjoint partition. A non-disjoint key in a bins dict is the sort of thing that gets summed by accident two quarters later.
- **ABSENT when the flag is off — never `0`, never `null`.** "We did not look" and "you own none" are different claims about the user's roster; collapsing the first into the second makes the card assert a fact nobody checked. The client gates on key presence and may never write `handcuff_rb ?? 0` — sabotage-proven in both languages.
- **The copy states the field, not a conclusion.** Rendered as *"RB2 on his NFL depth chart"*, never as a usage or value claim, because in a true committee the order-2 back may be a co-starter. That is the same honesty the approximation would have violated — the difference is that here the underlying fact is real and merely narrow.
**Alternatives considered:** *The value approximation `plan-remaining.md` proposed* — rejected for the reason that doc gives, and moot once the real field was found. *A new depth-chart dependency* — unnecessary; nothing is fetched that was not already being fetched. *Fold it into `trade.position_tiers`* — rejected: the tier flag changes every deck and this one changes one integer no engine path reads, so a deck regression must be revertible without also taking down a harmless label.
**Consequences:** Zero new dependency, zero new fetch, zero schema change — the columns have been sitting populated since before this feature existed. Staleness is inherited and stated on the card's terms: worst case ~24 h, and in-season charts churn faster than that (a Wednesday injury makes Tuesday's RB2 the RB1). Coverage is partial by design. **The lesson generalises:** `plan-remaining.md` §2 asserted an absence without grepping for it, and the assertion survived into a recommendation. Check the schema before concluding a feed does not exist.
**Status:** Active, **dark**. Flag not lit.
## D-130 — The Team Review `plan` Beat Is a Standing Summary of Every Finder Lever, Not a Session Receipt
**Date:** 2026-08-20 ([scope](../docs/feedback/items/369-plan-beat/scope.md), [code-walk](../docs/feedback/items/369-plan-beat/code-walk.md); branch `worktree-agent-a7bed877f805980b0`)
**Context:** Feedback #369 (`mattmurf77`, screen `TeamReview`): *"The plan summary page only shows window.. it's a good page intent but needs more detail. I think we just show the full set of adjustments a user can make with the trade finder."* The beat shipped as a receipt over `done.current`, a `useRef` set of actions committed in the current mount, so skipping a beat left its lever invisible. Tracing it turned up a second, sharper cause: **the depth beat's write had never once succeeded.** It posted `{acquire_positions, trade_away_positions}` with no `team_outlook`; `POST /api/league/preferences` 400s without one (`server.py:15788-15790`) and `apiRequest` throws on non-2xx (`api/client.ts:553`), so the `await` threw, `done.current.add('positions_set')` on the next line never ran, the empty `catch` swallowed it and no analytics fired. `positions_set` was permanently false. The window beat's payload *does* carry `team_outlook`, so it alone returned 200 — which is literally why the page showed only the window. `plan-remaining.md` §3 diagnosed the receipt design and did not know about the 400; that section has been corrected in place.
**Decision:** Rebuild the beat around a different question — every lever the trade finder exposes and where the user stands on each — sourced from **saved** state, never session state. `GET /api/league/preferences` on the `['league-prefs', leagueId]` key with `refetchOnMount: 'always'`, `GET /api/league/asset-prefs` for the three player rules, `AsyncStorage[ftf:trades:fairness_on]` for fairness, the `useFinderTargets` store for pinned players. Levers that live in `TradesScreen` `useState` (trade idea, focus) are **named with their home and no value claimed** rather than given a fabricated default. Separately, backfill `team_outlook` inside `savePrefs` — the single write site every beat funnels through — so the depth beat's write lands; `...patch` spreads after the backfill so an explicit value still wins.
**Alternatives considered:** *Keep the receipt and fix only the 400* — rejected: it makes the page correct but still answers "what you changed", which is not what the operator asked for. *Read the levers off the team-review payload (`data.depth.acquire_positions`)* — rejected and now guarded against: that snapshot is taken at screen mount under `staleTime: 60_000`, i.e. **before** this session's own writes, so it would reproduce the bug in a new shape. *Fix the 400 in the depth beat's footer* — rejected on ownership and on generality: `savePrefs` is the one place all beats write through, and a sibling agent owned the depth surface this session. *`team_review_plan_edited` as a new analytics event* — rejected; `team_review_action_taken` already carries `beat`, and the taxonomy's own comment block (`analytics_taxonomy.py:413-423`) warns that splitting a series is the harm.
**Consequences:** No new route, no new flag, no schema change, no payload change — the beat reads two existing GETs and writes the existing POST. Confined to a screen that only mounts under the already-lit `trades.team_review`, which stays the kill switch. **`team_review_action_taken{action:'positions_set'}` has never been emitted in production**; it starts producing data on the next client release, so its history is not a baseline. `check-team-review.js` grew 7 → 13 assertions, all nine sabotages red-then-restored. One pre-existing assertion (5b, the bare-playoff-percentage guard) was written as a whole-file `!/accessibilityLabel/` escape hatch and would have gone **vacuous** the moment this change added an `accessibilityLabel` anywhere on the screen — it was rewritten per-occurrence in the same commit. Requires a client release; not in build 122.
**Status:** Active. Committed, not pushed, not merged — parent agent integrates.

## D-131 — Hybrid Edit Surface: the Plan Beat Edits `league_preferences` in Place and Only Names the Rest
**Date:** 2026-08-20 (companion to D-130)
**Context:** Having decided the beat shows every lever (D-130), the open question was how many of them it should let the user *change* there. The verified inventory is twelve: three in `league_preferences`, three in `asset_preferences`, one in `AsyncStorage`, four in `TradesScreen` React state / the `useFinderTargets` store, plus the active league. (`trade.avoid_positions` is **not** on `origin/main` — it lives on `feat/jon-360-362`; no dependency was taken on it and the position block is laid out so it drops in as one more labelled line.)
**Decision:** Edit **`team_outlook`, `acquire_positions`, `trade_away_positions`** in place, autosaving the full triple per tap through the existing `saveLeaguePreferences` → `POST /api/league/preferences` path — the same #236 contract the Trade DNA sheet uses. Every other lever is displayed with its current standing where readable and its home named. The one exception is the scoped partner: the beat does not edit it, but its footer now *applies* what the partners beat already recorded, via the #330 `setHandoff` store — the partners beat had only ever set local state, so the plan beat's "I've already pointed the finder at it" was a false claim.
**Alternatives considered:** *Edit everything* — rejected: the asset lists would need a second `asset_preferences` writer racing the deck's own toggles for no gain, and fairness / trade idea / focus / pinned players live in another screen's `useState`, which cannot be written across a navigation boundary without inventing a cross-screen state layer for a closing beat. *Display-only plus a "Review all trade settings" deep link* (the option `plan-remaining.md` §3 guessed was "probably right") — rejected: a bare receipt plus a link under-delivers against *"show the full set of adjustments"*, and there is no route param that opens the DNA sheet, so the link would have meant editing `TradesScreen`. *A shared preferences store both screens read* — rejected as a speculative abstraction for one page.
**Consequences:** The blast radius is exactly the existing write path; nothing new can be written from this screen, pinned by guard assertion 9 (no `setAssetPref` in the file) and assertion 10 (exactly one `saveLeaguePreferences` call site, always carrying `team_outlook`). The plan beat becomes the recovery surface for a user who skipped the window or depth beat — they can still set both from here. `PLAN_POSITIONS` is `QB RB WR TE PICK`, wider than the depth beat's `CORE`, because `PICK` is a real value in these arrays and a page claiming to show every lever cannot drop one; the depth beat correctly stays on `CORE` because it is talking about startable bodies. **Uncorrected cosmetic side effect:** the handoff auto-run emits `find_trades_tapped{source:'league_offer'}` (`TradesScreen.tsx:2408`), so a Team-Review-originated auto-run is misattributed; fixing it means extending the handoff shape and editing `TradesScreen.tsx`, which this agent did not own.
**Status:** Active.

## D-140 — The Window Is One Re-Weighted Composite, Age at 40 %; the Cuts Do Not Move
**Date:** 2026-08-20 ([scope](../docs/feedback/items/372-window-composite/scope.md), [code-walk](../docs/feedback/items/372-window-composite/code-walk.md); branch `claude/372-window-composite`)
**Context:** Feedback #372 (`mattmurf77`, screen `TeamReview`), the **third** report on this one beat after #365 and #371: *"The logic is still too simple… age distribution alone is not a strong enough of a signal. We calculate starter dynasty value. Let's incorporate that and playoff likelihood. The age distribution can stay but make it a lighter driver for the evaluation."* Two prior builds each added a signal beside the age model and left the age model's weight alone; a third report is the evidence that adding a fourth term was not the fix. Measured on 12 real prod leagues / 156 teams, **the legacy vector calls 65 % of every team in production a rebuilder** (101 / 26 not_sure / 29 contender) — a systematic skew, not a bad case.
**Decision:** Re-weight the whole vector behind `trade.outlook_composite` (**default OFF**): vet `1.00 → 0.40`, youth `1.00 → 0.40`, pick `2.00` unchanged, **new** starter-value index `+0.60` capped ±0.50, **new** playoff index `+0.40` capped ±1.00, net-firsts `0.10` unchanged behind its own flag. Four sub-rulings:
- **Both age terms move together.** `vet_age` 27 and `youth_age` 26 are *adjacent*, so every aged player is one or the other and the pair is close to one rescaled quantity. Halving only one would tilt the model, not lighten it. Their joint contribution drops 60 %, which is the operator's instruction made arithmetic, and it is pinned to that exact ratio rather than to "lower".
- **Starter value, not roster value.** Total roster value is already the standing beat's number and it rewards hoarding — a rebuilder sitting on nine young WR4s can out-total a contender. `starter_index = share × num_teams − 1` (0 = an average starting lineup), summed off the `compute_power_rankings` output the route already holds, so `team_review.py`'s "this module computes nothing new" rule survives intact; the rescaling by league size is what lets **one** weight be correct at every league shape.
- **THE CUTS DO NOT MOVE.** ±0.08, unchanged. The distribution is the reason: after re-weighting, the same 156 teams spread 62 / 40 / 54, so a cut move would be tuning on top of a fix rather than part of one. Moving them later is a headline, not a footnote, and `test_the_cuts_did_not_move` makes that break a test.
- **Same two invariants as D-110, extended.** INV-372: flag OFF ⇒ both new kwargs are accepted and ignored, byte-identical to `c00a9a6` for every caller, pinned against goldens captured from a `git archive` of that tree rather than re-derived. INV-372b: flag ON but with no **applied** starter signal ⇒ the **legacy** vector still scores, because a re-weighting that halves age without putting anything in its place is not a model, it is a quieter one. Starter value can only be summed off a league-wide power-rankings call, which no generation path makes, so `trade_gen_v2`, the mock draft and the outlook seed are structurally unreachable — lighting this moves the window beat and not one deck.
**Alternatives considered:** *Add starter value as a fourth term and leave age at 1.00* — rejected; that is what #365 and #371 each did and it is what produced a third report. *Drop `youth_share` entirely and keep `vet_share`* — rejected: adjacent-threshold siblings, so dropping one converts a symmetric signal into a directional thumb. *Move the cuts to compensate for the smaller score magnitudes* — rejected on the measured distribution. *Reuse the `infer_w_*` keys for the composite weights* — rejected: those are what every engine caller still scores with, so reusing them would mean the composite could not be tuned without moving the engine. *Verify against `data/trade_finder.db`* — rejected outright: that corpus **does not contain FFV3**, which is exactly how #365's session drew a backwards conclusion; every number here is read-only prod.
**Consequences:** On FFV3 the operator goes from `−0.4867 rebuilder` to `+0.2009 contender` — his starters are worth 82 % more than the league mean while he holds essentially no pick capital, the all-in reading the age model could not see. The model also disagrees with itself in the other direction, which is the better sanity check: `PaulSm3nis` is a legacy **contender** on age alone while owning the league's *worst* starting lineup, and the composite calls him a rebuilder. `window.model` re-states `w_vet_share` at 0.40, so a client that hardcodes 1.00 now prints arithmetic that does not add up to the total beside it — D-101's third outing, guarded in both languages. The signal ships **both** `index_raw` (measured) and `index` (scored, clamped): the cap binds on real rosters, and a card printing only `index` would tell the FFV3 caller his starters are 50 % above the league mean when they are 82 % above — the model's number stated as the team's, which is D-101 from a new angle. Two dead assertions surfaced during sabotage and are recorded in scope §3.1: a refused-signal test that passed against a broken function because the helper zeroes its own index, and a mobile regex that required the literal word "starters" near a gate written `st.index`. Requires a client release; not in build 122.
**Status:** Active, **dark**. Flag not lit; graduation needs the TestFlight pass.

## D-141 — When the Composite Is Live, the Playoff Band Is Scored, Not Obeyed
**Date:** 2026-08-20 (companion to D-140)
**Context:** D-111 (`trades.window_from_odds`) makes the simulated playoff band **replace** `window.inferred`. D-140 makes the same band a **weighted term inside the score**. Both flags can be on at once, and naively that means one simulation drives the verdict twice — once at weight 0.40 and again at infinity.
**Decision:** The composite wins. `team_review.resolve_window_precedence(composite_applied, odds_source, odds_implied, roster_window)`: when the composite actually applied, return `("composite", roster_window)` and suppress the replacement; otherwise #371's behaviour is untouched, down to `source` staying `None` while its own flag is off. `window.source` gains a third value, `"composite"`, meaning `inferred == roster_inferred` by construction because nothing replaced anything. The odds term inside the composite **inherits** #371's admission rule rather than re-deriving it — the route hands `playoff_odds_signal` the refusal string `resolve_window_from_odds` produced — so "Sleeper-only" and "refused in preseason" (D-094) exist in exactly one place.
**Alternatives considered:** *Keep them fully independent* — rejected: that is the double-count, and it would put a `Playoff odds × 0.4` row in the arithmetic under a headline the arithmetic did not produce. *Have `window_from_odds` win* — rejected: a replacement discards the other four signals entirely, which is the opposite of what #372 asked for. *Read the band without requiring `trades.window_from_odds`* — rejected: that flag is the odds kill switch for this beat and must stay one; when it is off the composite's playoff term carries the fourth provenance value `odds_disabled` ("we did not ask", which is not "we asked and got nothing"), and that value deliberately does **not** leak into `window.odds_reason`'s vocabulary.
**Consequences:** The rule lives in `team_review.py` rather than inline in the Flask handler, for the reason that module already gives for `resolve_window_from_odds`: a precedence rule that only exists inside a request handler is a rule nothing can test. Extracting it is what made sabotage S7 possible at all. All four flag combinations are valid and the TestFlight checklist walks them.
**Status:** Active, **dark**.

## D-142 — The Counterparty Breaker Speaks the User's Own Decline Vocabulary, Stamps Before It Ever Renders, and Ships Only the Sentence
**Date:** 2026-08-21 ([plan suite](../docs/plans/counterparty-breaker/): PLAN / PRD / HLD / LLD + [scope](../docs/plans/counterparty-breaker/scope.md), [code-walk](../docs/plans/counterparty-breaker/code-walk.md), [readout spec](../docs/plans/counterparty-breaker/calibration-readout-spec.md); branch `claude/counterparty-breaker-plan`)
**Context:** The engine tells a user why *they* should want a trade and says nothing about why the other manager would refuse. Predicting that is a new *kind* of layer — it evaluates a deck the generators have already finished — and it is unusually easy to get wrong in three directions at once: a private vocabulary that can never be checked against reality, an evaluation seam that quietly perturbs the deck it was supposed to observe, and a payload that leaks the model's internals to the client. The doc suite (HLD D-1..D-11, LLD §1–§8) argues each of these at length; this entry records the three rulings that outlive it.
**Decision:** One decision in three parts, all shipped behind two default-OFF flags (`trade.breaker` = compute + stamp; `trade.breaker_narrative` = the on-card line, structurally requiring the first).
- **The objection vocabulary IS the decline taxonomy.** The breaker emits `database.PASS_REASON_LAYER2` codes — imported, not copied — plus exactly one registered extension, `roster_crunch`. Every code gains a **producer** so a shared enum stays attributable across sibling plans, and the extension is declared **unmeasurable** up front (no filed-reason anchor exists; scoring it against hand-coded `other_text` is forbidden) rather than being quietly precision-scored later.
- **v1 stamps and narrates; it does not touch the deck.** The seam runs post-mutation-stack, pre-ghost-split, and the only writes are two new card attributes. Filtering or demoting on a breaker verdict (product outcome 1) is **v2** and needs its own scope block, because it changes deck composition and would put the evaluator inside the interleave discipline that keeps the bake-off measurable.
- **The payload is narration-gated.** The client receives `{code, severity, sentence}` **only** for a card that actually narrated; during the dark-stamp window it receives no `breaker` key at all, and the viewer-seat shadow never serializes anywhere. Payload presence is therefore the client's entire gate — it re-checks no flag and switches on no code.
**Alternatives considered:** *Invent a breaker-specific objection vocabulary tuned to the predicates* — rejected: it reads better and can never be validated, since the only ground truth we collect is in the user's vocabulary; the join is worth more than the fit. *Score `roster_crunch` against hand-coded free text so every class has a graduation row* — rejected: hand-coding our own `other_text` into our own prediction's categories is a way to grade our own homework; the row stays honestly unpassable in v1. *Let the breaker demote or filter cards in v1* — rejected: it would make the evaluator a generator, and every ordering claim in the bake-off would need re-establishing. *Ship the objection vector to the client and gate rendering on the flag there* — rejected twice over: `other_player_keep` is scored but permanently dark (it would advertise that we read the counterparty's private keep-list), and a client-side eligibility re-implementation drifts from the server's the first time a floor moves. *Render the line through `build_narrative`* — rejected: no client renders `TradeCard.narrative` today, so the sentence would have shipped invisible.
**Consequences:** Calibration needs no new instrumentation — `features_json.breaker` ⨝ `trade_pass_reasons` on `impression_id` is the whole measurement, which is why the [calibration-readout spec](../docs/plans/counterparty-breaker/calibration-readout-spec.md) could be **preregistered before the flag ever lights** (HLD D-6 / R-3) with its boundaries and per-class gates fixed in advance. The primary population is the **viewer-seat shadow**, labeled proxy validation with its selection caveat, because the counterparty-seat population is ≈0 today. Three conventions land in [LLD.md](LLD.md) (vocabulary + producer, uniform-key stamps whose empty value is a marker object rather than a bare null, narration-gated payloads); the "not a client invariant" ruling is recorded as its own row in [cross-client-invariants](../docs/cross-client-invariants.md) so a future per-code icon has to argue with a written decision. Rollback is deploy-free and laddered: narrative flag off → per-class narrate switches / `breaker_min_severity` via `scripts/set_knob.py` → compute flag off → revert. Nothing persisted needs cleanup: stamps are version-stamped (`brk-1`) inert serve-time facts, never backfilled, and a readout that cannot filter on `ver` is a readout that must not run.
**Status:** Active, **dark**. Both flags false; `trade.breaker` graduates on the coverage bar + one readout, `trade.breaker_narrative` on the operator TestFlight pass (PRD §8.3) — **unrun**, and no runtime evidence exists for this feature yet.


## D-143 — Package Benchmark + Sweetener Ship as a PAIR; Rollback Is Both Knobs Together
**Date:** 2026-08-22 (PR #162 `d42872f`; evidence docs/plans/package-benchmark-sweetener/ + TEST_LEDGER prod-replay addendum)
**Context:** The benchmark fix (trade-best depth benchmarking) and the gap auto-sweetener shipped together. The prod-boards replay's threshold-0 control showed the fix ALONE regresses the over-line share (v3 6.7→11.3%, v2 9.9→15.2%) — honest pricing exposes gaps the old math hid; the sweetener is what nets them below baseline (→2.8%/5.1%).
**Decision:** The two are one product change. `sweetener_gap_threshold ≤ 0` is NOT a rollback lever for the fix; any revert flips `package_bench_trade_wide ≤ 0` AND `sweetener_gap_threshold ≤ 0` together. Arm A pins both at 0.0 (byte-identity proven; golden un-recaptured — operator-ratified deviation, re-capture becomes mandatory if the legacy path is ever deleted). Arm C benched at ship until its sweetener extension lands.
**Status:** Active. Live since 2026-08-22.

## D-147 — Negative-Results Memory Is BUILT and Dark: the Code Is Complete, the Rollout Is Two Operator Flips
**Date:** 2026-08-22 (three build waves on `claude/vigilant-spence-8583f5`; formal record [ADR-015](../docs/adr/adr-015-negmem-soft-prior-not-fourth-filter.md); operator rulings 2026-08-22 in [scope.md §6-RULINGS](../docs/plans/negative-results-memory/scope.md))
**Context:** The doc suite (PRD/HLD/LLD FINAL, dual sign-off) closed with the build gate OPEN after the operator ruled all three surfaced decisions: D1 family-level soft prior YES, D2 layer-2 boundary SEED ONLY, D3 privacy posture (a) FULL layer 2 — the last one knowingly against the recommendation. The build then ran as three waves against the LLD's 27-test plan.
**Decision:** v1 is **complete and dark**. `backend/negmem.py` (leaf: stdlib + feature_flags + database only), the full triple registration (flag `trade.negmem` + six `negmem_*` knobs seeded and pinned in `MODEL_A_PROFILE`), all four consultation seams (serving stack, gen_v2, fit, and both `bakeoff_runner` arm adapters), the features-assembly stamp, the M2 feed at both gen_v2 call sites, the two operator scripts, the two readout-pack SQL files and the docs/ADR rows are on the branch. **Nothing about the rollout is a code change:** lighting it is two operator actions — add the league to `config/negmem_leagues.json` and flip `trade.negmem` — taken at a bake-off ROUND BOUNDARY per GR3/[ADR-014](../docs/adr/adr-014-bakeoff-serving-rounds.md). The ON-condition is deliberately **both**, so shipping the code changes nothing until someone decides it should. Design rationale for the soft-prior-not-a-filter shape, the coarse `(partner × reason family)` key, and the recorded consequences of ruling D3(a) — including the `delete_user_data` obligation any future *persistence* incurs — live in ADR-015, not here.
**Alternatives considered:** *Ship the allowlist pre-populated with the operator's league* — rejected: it merges a code change and a rollout decision into one commit, and the round-boundary rule would then be satisfied by accident or not at all. *Graduate M1 and M2 independently* — rejected for v1: they ride one map and one flag, and separating their lifecycles would double the rollback ladder for a feed that is a seed, not a model.
**Consequences:** The evidence that exists is **structural**: the full suite is green with the negmem tests carrying named sabotages (counts and sabotage names in [TEST_LEDGER.md](TEST_LEDGER.md)). The evidence that does **not** exist is runtime — no deck has ever been generated with this flag on for a real league, and the [TestFlight checklist](../docs/plans/negative-results-memory/testflight-checklist.md) is written and **unrun**. Do not describe the feature as validated. Two ordinary-looking mistakes are pre-recorded because both look like success: killing M2 through a per-arm overlay leaves the feed populated (only the GLOBAL `gen2_accept_prior_strength = 0` empties it), and a stamp-rate query returning zero rows means an empty allowlist, not failing builds.
**Status:** Active, **dark**. Flag false, allowlist empty. Graduation is the pre-registered RFPS rule (PRD §8.3) evaluated once at window close.
## D-144 — Receipts Grades Are Append-Only and Version-Stamped; a Correction Is a Regrade, Never an Edit
**Date:** 2026-08-21 (Receipts P1, [docs/plans/receipts/](../docs/plans/receipts/))
**Context:** Receipts publishes the graded track record of our own suggestions. Its entire value is that the number is checkable — which means a user has to be able to believe we did not move the goalposts after seeing the outcome. "We wouldn't do that" is not a mechanism.
**Decision:** `receipts_grades` and `receipts_grade_runs` are APPEND-ONLY, keyed `(impression_id, window_days, grader_version)`. There is no UPDATE or DELETE path for either table anywhere in the codebase — `database.py` exposes insert + select helpers only, and a test greps both modules and fails if one appears. A wrong grade is corrected by bumping `GRADER_VERSION` and regrading the cohort; superseded rows are RETAINED, reads pin the max version by **numeric suffix** (`receipts-10` > `receipts-2`, never lexicographic), and the screen footnotes the regrade. Three further preregistration rules ride the same enforcement: no engine import, no live-value read for valuation (sole exemption: the module's own frozen pick weights, coverage/pick-share only), no asset reconstruction from `trade_hash`.
**Alternatives considered:** UPDATE-in-place (destroys the audit trail — "we corrected it" becomes indistinguishable from "we rewrote history"); never-fix (ships a known-wrong screen forever).
**Consequences:** Regrades cost storage, which is free at this scale. Two `grader_version` bumps inside one month is an explicit abort trigger (PLAN A-4): stop and re-review the grader design rather than iterating on corrections.
**Status:** Active. Built dark 2026-08-21.

## D-145 — The Receipts Metric Is Swap Edge on Consensus, Not an Acquire-Side Percentage
**Date:** 2026-08-21 (Receipts, HLD D-1/D-2; three adversarial review rounds)
**Context:** The obvious framing — "the player you'd have acquired is up 14%" — is the one every competitor could also produce, and it is close to meaningless: in a rising market every acquire side is up. It also fails on our own data, because the card's frozen `give_value`/`receive_value` may be the user's PERSONAL board (`user_value_basis`), so comparing them to later consensus manufactures movement out of a basis mismatch.
**Decision:** The metric is **swap edge** = `receive-side consensus delta − give-side consensus delta`, both endpoints read from `player_value_history` and never from the frozen card. The give side is the market control. The claim made for it is exactly as strong as the algebra supports and no stronger: uniform multiplicative drift yields `edge = m × (serve-time imbalance)` (≈0 for fairness-gated packages, NOT exactly 0); uniform additive drift yields `edge = d × (n_receive − n_give)` (exactly 0 only for equal-cardinality shapes, `−d` for a 2x1). Residuals are disclosed per shape cell. Picks contribute delta 0 — their prices are static code seeds repriced by our own commits, so grading them would grade our deploys.
**Alternatives considered:** Acquire-side % with a `beat` flag (rejected: measures the market, and a standalone acquire-side % is now banned copy); grading against personal boards from `elo_history` (rejected as permanently endogenous — a user's swipes are partly the echo of our own suggestion, HL-2).
**Consequences:** Harder to explain than "+14%", and the honest version is a smaller-sounding number. The drift-cancellation claim was overstated in the first draft ("cancels for every shape") and corrected in review; the SIGN of the disclosed 2x1 residual was also wrong at round 3, and is now test-pinned in both directions — a flipped sign would have rendered every win as a loss.
**Status:** Active. Built dark 2026-08-21.

## D-146 — Draft Picks Price at Their Own Draft Slot; the Per-User Mode, Its Route and Its Flag Are Retired

**Date:** 2026-08-21 ([scope](../docs/plans/slot-pricing-unconditional/scope.md)); closes [Q-023](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) in full, rules and defers [Q-026](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md)

**Operator ruling.** *"Market slots should be default and not an opt-in or even an option to flip. Aligned that future picks stay default for now."* Clarified the same day: "market slots" means **each pick holding *real value rather than generic*** — true per-slot pricing, not merely a market-flavoured round curve.

**Decision.** An owned pick's engine value is a three-step waterfall in `pick_values.priced_pool_value`, resolved at read time, for every user, with no flag read, no session read and no DB read:

1. **The pick's OWN slot price** — `market_pick_slot_value(season, round, slot)`, DP's `values.csv` row for e.g. `"2026 Pick 1.03"` — whenever D-090 resolves a real slot.
2. **The round curve** — `market_pick_pool_value`, the mid-tercile basis — when no slot resolves.
3. **The stored ladder `pool_value`** — when DP publishes nothing at all.

**Slot resolution is D-090's, reused rather than reimplemented**: `server._league_slot_order` (DB-backed, 60 s cache, once per *league*) → `pick_slots.slot_for`, which already refuses future seasons (#273), unknown rosters, malformed blobs and unverifiable snake reversals. `priced_pool_value` runs per *pick* and resolves nothing itself; the slot is passed in. The same resolution drives the LABEL, so a card cannot say "2026 1.03" while charging for a generic first.

**Why step 2 survives as more than a curiosity.** It is what every future-year pick gets — and it needs no branch to be so, because DP publishes per-slot rows only for the current class, so step 1 returns `None` for 2027+ by itself. The operator's "future picks stay default for now" therefore falls out of the *data*, not out of a special case that could rot.

**The three things the ruling deleted rather than defaulted:**

- **The setting.** `users.pick_pricing_mode` becomes dead data (kept — additive-schema rule; a restore would need no migration). The mobile Settings row is deleted from both the legacy flat screen and the v2 `TradeValuesSection`, with its client, its state and its `pick_pricing_mode_changed` emitter.
- **The route.** `PUT /api/settings/pick-pricing` → **410 Gone** for any body; `GET` → 200 `{mode: "market_slots", retired: true}` (the fixed state, not the dead column) so builds in the field render honestly instead of hiding the control on a 404. First retired route in this repo; the convention is in LLD § "Retiring a per-user setting".
- **The flag.** `trade.slot_pricing` stays in `FLAG_KEYS` at `true` and is **never read**. Deleting it would force a six-file change to satisfy `test_release_flags_mirror_features_json`, make the key vanish from `/api/feature-flags` for shipped builds, and reinterpret any stored override row as an unknown key.

**The spread this buys, 1QB, 2026 round 1** (pinned snapshot): 1.01 **4867.1**, 1.05 2343.2, 1.08 1435.5, 1.12 **820.8** — against one round price of 1859.5 and one ladder rung of 2117.0 that every 2026 first used to be charged at. A 1.01 is **5.9× a 1.12** and **2.30×** the old rung. Superflex is dearer again (1.01 = 6181.1). Rounds 2–4 deflate at almost every slot.

**So the headline is DISPERSION, not deflation.** The round curve alone (the intermediate step this branch also built) deflates everything — 2026 1st −12.2 %, 2028 1st −40.3 %, 2026 2nd −28.4 %, with deep-future 4ths the lone inversion at +16.6 %. Per-slot then *re-inflates the top of round 1 past the old ladder* and pushes the bottom well below it. Both regimes are shown side by side on a real served card in the scope block §7.1.

**What did NOT move.** `GENERIC_PICK_SEEDS`, the tier ladder, the absolute tier BANDS, `tier_config.json`, and `draft_picks.pool_value` (never written; pricing is read-time). Owned-pick tier BADGES do move — a badge reflects the served value (D-320-2) and the served value moved. Per-slot is where that finally bites: a 1.01 and a 1.12 now badge in genuinely different bands.

**Consequence the operator should see: `picks.slot_labels` became a PRICING flag.** `_league_slot_order` returns `None` when it is off, so turning that flag off drops every pick from step 1 to step 2 without a deploy. This is an accident of reusing D-090 and it is also the **only deploy-free lever** over the larger half of the repricing. Recommended disposition: accept and document it (labels and prices moving together is coherent — you never show "2026 1.01" while charging generic). The alternative, a separate `trade.pick_slot_pricing` flag, means adding a flag on the day we retired one, and the ruling says pricing is not "an option to flip". **Needs a call — scope §6 waiver 1.**

**Rejected: a `model_config` rollback knob for the mode itself.** D-079's precedent argues for one. Rejected because any such knob is *"an option to flip"*, which the ruling forbids in terms. Reverting to the ladder is revert-and-redeploy.

**Rejected: clamping rounds in `market_pick_slot_value`.** `market_pick_pool_value` clamps to DP's published round 5 so a round-9 pick still gets a price. The per-slot function deliberately does not: a round-9 slot has no published row and no honest analogue, so it returns `None` and rides step 2's clamp. One clamp, one place, no drift.

**Evidence.** **156 golden assertions across seven golden files pass with zero edits, twice** — once after the unconditional change and again after the per-slot extension, each time run in isolation before any fixture was touched. Arm A pins every input as a literal and its picks are Elo-map pseudo-assets, so it never constructs a `draft_picks` row and never reaches `priced_pool_value`; immune by construction. No kill-value pin, no re-capture. Gate interactions (overpay/R1, `sweetener_gap_threshold` 1539.0, `pick_gap_ok`) pass untouched — note the spread now straddles that threshold, with a 1.01 (4867.1) far above it and a 1.12 (820.8) far below, where the ladder put every first at 2117.0 on one side.

Three test files moved, all re-derived from the new pricing functions with inputs pinned as literals; no tolerance widened. One structural guard was **made stricter while being widened**: `test_m6_02b_pick_values_reads_dp_only_through_the_m6b_seam` gained a second legitimate DP reader, so it was rewritten from a source-line comparison to an AST walk asserting the reader set is *exactly* `{market_pick_pool_value, market_pick_slot_value}` in both directions, plus a module-level-import refusal. Sabotage-verified: adding a third reader fails it.

**Consequence worth naming: this change made pytest non-hermetic and required the repo's first `conftest.py`.** Unconditional pricing put a live DP fetch on the hot path of every pick-touching test — measured, a 2029 1st priced at 1459.4 from real network data. With network the suite is flaky-by-calendar; without it, it passes for the wrong reason. `backend/tests/conftest.py` `setdefault`s `FTF_DP_PICK_VALUES_FILE` to the checked-in snapshot, which already carries full per-slot coverage (2026, rounds 1–5, slots 01–12) and zero future-season slot rows — so it exercises both step 1 and step 2 without extension.

**Status:** Active — **SHIPPED** same day as PR [#167](https://github.com/mattmurf77/fantasy-trade-finder/pull/167) → `main` `3192d13`. Waiver 1 (slot_labels-as-pricing-flag) shipped under the recommended-accept disposition; operator veto window open. Renumbered from the draft's D-144 (taken by a sibling session — G-048 again).

## D-148 — One Pick, One Price: Every League Surface Reads the Engine's Waterfall

**Date:** 2026-08-21 ([scope](../../plans/league-pick-value-alignment/scope.md)); **closes [Q-026](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) in full**; opens [Q-027](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md)

**Operator ruling.** *"I want the league values to reflect the same pick values.. But let's defer that until after finishing this one."* The deferral expired when the per-slot ship merged as PR [#167](https://github.com/mattmurf77/fantasy-trade-finder/pull/167) → `main` `3192d13`. This was a sequenced commitment, never an open question.

**The disagreement it ends.** [D-146](DECISIONS.md) put the three-step waterfall into the ENGINE and knowingly left two league surfaces on the stored `draft_picks.pool_value` (slot-pricing scope §6 waiver 2). Dark, that was invisible. Live under per-slot pricing it was stark: a **2026 1.01 read 2117.0 on the Power Rankings screen and 4867.1 inside a trade card** — 2.3×, about the single most valuable asset a team can hold, and it badged differently on each screen because a badge follows the value it is served (D-320-2).

**Decision.** Every site that displays or aggregates an owned pick's value calls **one** helper:

```python
def _priced_pick_value(p, slot_order, scoring_format) -> float:
    return priced_pool_value(p, scoring_format=scoring_format,
                             slot=pick_slots.slot_for(slot_order, p.get("season"),
                                                      p.get("round"),
                                                      p.get("original_roster_id")))
```

Five call sites, no sixth — `_roster_eveners`, `_trade_evaluate_impl`, `get_league_picks`, `_owned_pick_assets`, `_power_picks_by_owner`. Two of those were already priced (D-146) and were refactored onto the seam without behaviour change; three were genuinely aligned.

**The helper is the decision, not an implementation detail.** The five sites had been drifting *because* each held its own copy of one expression. Extracting the seam makes "the same waterfall with the same slot resolution" a structural property, guarded by a bidirectional AST walk (`test_league_pick_value_alignment.py`): one test asserts `priced_pool_value` is reached from exactly one function, another that the seam's callers are exactly the five. **Sabotage-verified three ways**, including one sabotage that is *behaviourally identical* (inlining the same expression at a call site) — it still fails, which is the point.

**Three surfaces moved, and one of them was a real defect.**

- **`GET /api/league/picks`** — `pool_value` on the wire is now the priced value, and `tier` follows it. No client change: `mobile/src/components/InLeagueCalculator.tsx:233` already read `p.pool_value` as its base.
- **`_power_picks_by_owner`** (Power Rankings + the ADR-011 history snapshot).
- **`_roster_eveners`** — **a defect, not merely an inconsistency.** Both call sites live inside `_trade_evaluate_impl`, whose `gap` is computed from priced picks, while the candidates were sized against the stored ladder. A one-tap "add their 2026 1.01" was offered as closing a 2117.0 hole the same response charged 4867.1 for.

**Badges move, and that is the ruling working.** The bands are byte-identical (`tier_config.json` + five client mirrors untouched) and so is the inverse (`value_to_elo`, D-088). Measured on a 12-team 2026–2028 grid: **50 of 96 picks re-band** — the 1.01 up to `firsts_2`, the 1.08–1.12 down to `second`, all twelve 2026 thirds and fourths down a band, and all twelve 2028 firsts from `first_1` to `second`.

**The number the operator will actually notice: Power Rankings totals DEFLATE.** Per-roster pick capital moves **+12.4 % (the roster picking 1.01) to −40.3 % (1.12), monotonically by slot, and −22.1 % league-wide.** The dispersion story is true *within* 2026 round 1; across a whole roster the round curve dominates, because DP decays future firsts hard (2028 1st: 2117.0 → 1263.0) where D-079's ladder held every first flat. These are the engine's prices as of `3192d13` — this decision does not create them, it stops one screen from disagreeing with them.

**Named consequence: an ADR-011 time-series boundary at this merge.** `roster_history.team_value` / `team_value_picks` are fed by `_power_picks_by_owner`, so the series steps here and is not comparable across the date for a pick-holding team; the Wrapped/recap and trends consumers read across it. **Nothing historical is recomputed**, and that is verified rather than argued: `roster_history` has no pricing path of its own — it is handed `picks_by_owner` and stores it — pinned by a test asserting the module contains no `priced_pool_value`, no `_priced_pick_value` and no `pick_pool_value` call.

**What did NOT move.** `GENERIC_PICK_SEEDS`, the tier ladder, the tier BANDS, `tier_config.json`, `pick_pool_value` itself, and `draft_picks.pool_value` (still sync-written, still never rewritten — it is explicitly the waterfall's step 3 and the entire safety net when DP is unreachable). No schema change, no flag change, no client change (`git diff origin/main -- mobile web extension` = zero files).

**Deliberately NOT aligned, and this needs a call — the pick-SHARE ratios.** `_user_pick_share` and the trade job's opponent shares sum the legacy `pick_value` column into a 0–1 ratio feeding the contend/rebuild classifier. Left alone because (a) nothing displays it — it is an inference input, not a surface; (b) aligning it changes which decks get generated, a behaviour change nobody commissioned and one the golden set cannot catch (arm A builds no `draft_picks` rows); (c) it reads a different column on a scale already documented as not client-facing. **Operator-visible consequence:** the app's guess at whether you are contending still counts every 2026 first as the same asset. Scope §6 waiver 3.

**Rejected: fixing the non-12-team board/engine mismatch here.** DP publishes one 12-team curve; the Draft Room maps smaller leagues onto it by percentile (`_basis_slot`) and `market_pick_slot_value` does not, so a 10-team league's last first *displays* as the 1.12 (820.8) and *prices* as the 1.10 (1069.8). Rejected because the fix reprices the ENGINE in every non-12-team league — outside a ruling about aligning surfaces to the engine. Pinned by a test that asserts both mappings and the exact gap, and raised as **Q-027**.

**Rejected: giving `/api/league/picks` a new key and leaving `pool_value` stored.** It would have kept the two numbers visible side by side and needed a five-client migration to decide which one to render — i.e. it would have shipped the disagreement into the clients instead of ending it.

**Evidence.** Golden set run **in isolation before any fixture was touched, and again after**: 29 tests / 156 assertions across four golden files, **zero edits, both times**; arm A's immunity verified rather than assumed. Full suite **3986 passed, 1 skipped** (baseline 3969). Three test files re-derived from the pricing functions with literal inputs, **no tolerance widened**; `test_league_picks_tier.py` grew 7 → 12 and re-verified all three of its original sabotages against the new prices, adding a fourth. One fixture had to be *reshaped* rather than renumbered: D-084's "one 3rd separates the literal and dollar label scales" trap collapsed under the new prices (both scales now round to ≈0.5 firsts), so `_PICKS_306` was re-derived to three thirds, which restores the divergence — the file explains why two would not do.

**Status:** Active — **SHIPPED** as PR [#169](https://github.com/mattmurf77/fantasy-trade-finder/pull/169) → `main` `70ae4f4` (CI green incl. mobile-typecheck, which the building agent could not run locally). Scope §6 waivers 1 (slot_labels lever now app-wide), 2 (Q-027) and 3 (pick shares) ship under recommended-accept; operator veto window open.

## D-149 — The Feedback Cap Is Raised, Not Removed; and the Client Holds Save Rather Than Truncating

**Date:** 2026-08-22 ([scope](../docs/plans/feedback-capture-cap/scope.md)); prompted by an operator report lost in the field, [G-055](GOTCHAS.md)

**What happened.** A long in-app feedback note was silently destroyed: `POST /api/feedback` refused it with `400 text_too_long`, and nothing on the client said so — no counter, no cap awareness, and an `onSave` that cleared the draft without ever checking the result. The note survived on-device (AsyncStorage is written before the POST) but was invisible unless you knew to open the inbox.

**Decision 1 — raise the cap to 8000, keep the check.** The tempting fix is to delete the validation. Rejected: `POST /api/feedback` is decorated `@_gate_unverified_write` but **accepts anonymous submissions by design** ("External testers' submissions land regardless of sign-in state"), so the length bound is the only thing standing between an unauthenticated caller and an arbitrarily large body. 8000 is ~4× the longest note in the corpus. `app_feedback.text` is a SQLAlchemy `Text` column — unbounded in SQLite and Postgres alike — so this is validation, never storage, and no migration exists to run.

**Decision 2 — soft cap on the client, NOT `maxLength`.** A `maxLength` on the compose input is the obvious guard and it is wrong here: React Native drops keystrokes past the limit with no signal and, worse, **silently truncates a paste** — which is the single most likely way an 8000-character note is entered. That is the same class of loss as the 400 we are fixing, moved earlier and made harder to notice. Instead the user types freely, a counter reddens past the cap and names the exact overshoot, and Save is *held* until the note is trimmed. **The user's text is never modified by the app.**

**Decision 3 — `add()` tells the truth, and the draft outlives a failure.** `useFeedback.add` previously fired the POST into a detached `void (async () => …)()` IIFE and returned the pre-sync item, so the resolved value was *always* `synced: false` and no caller could distinguish delivery from rejection. It now awaits the sync and resolves the item's real state. `onSave` clears the draft only inside the success branch; on failure it keeps the text, keeps the sheet open, and says *"saved on this device, but not sent yet"* with the inbox route named. It deliberately does **not** say the note was lost, because it was not.

**Consequence, accepted:** Save now blocks for the round-trip (up to the 15s client timeout on a dead network) instead of returning instantly. You cannot report an outcome you have not waited for. Covered by a spinner and a disabled Cancel; `FeedbackSheet` is `add()`'s only caller, so nothing else changes.

**One number, pinned across the seam.** The cap lives in `backend/server.py:FEEDBACK_TEXT_MAX` (authority) and is mirrored at `mobile/src/api/feedback.ts:FEEDBACK_TEXT_MAX`. `mobile/tests/check-feedback-capture.js` resolves both by name and asserts equality, so a one-sided change fails CI. **This caught a real mismatch on day one** — for one window during the parallel build the client sat at 8000 while the server was still at 2000, and the assertion fired with the right diagnosis.

**Deploy order is load-bearing and CI cannot police it.** The guard blocks a mismatched *merge*, not a mismatched *deploy*. **Backend first is safe** (an old client simply caps itself lower); **client first reproduces the original defect** for notes of 2001–8000 characters. The backend half ships via Render immediately; the client half waits for a TestFlight build, which is the correct order by construction — but any future change to this number must respect it deliberately.

**Rejected: an offline/failed-submit analytics event.** `obs.api_events` is on and `record_inbound` writes failures **unsampled**, with `/api/feedback` absent from `EXCLUDED_ROUTES` — so every `text_too_long` 400 is already captured with a closed error code. A new event would duplicate an existing signal. Note the limit of that consolation: it captures 400s **going forward only**. There is no server-side record of a rejected submission, so how many notes this cap ate historically is **unknowable**.

**Status:** Active — backend shipped; the client half awaits a TestFlight build.

## D-150 — The Merged Calculator Is Built Dark in Five Waves; Three Of Its Rulings Cost Less Than Planned

**Date:** 2026-08-22 ([plan](../docs/feedback/items/384-calc-finder-merge/plan.md), [status](../docs/feedback/items/384-calc-finder-merge/status.md)); entry point feedback #384, canonical for #310/#379/#380, touches #333

**Context.** #384 is the report the 2000-char cap destroyed ([D-149](DECISIONS.md), [G-055](GOTCHAS.md)) — 2,803 characters specifying a merged Find-a-Trade/calculator surface plus a fifteen-beat guided tour. The operator ruled ten open questions at triage and four more at plan review, then said "continue through all waves."

**Decision 1 — the merged layout is one flag, and flag-off is proven, not asserted.** `calc.merged_layout` (default false) gates every new surface. The guard does not grep for gates; it **excises every flag-gated region by brace balancing and asserts no merged-only testID survives in the remainder**. What remains is by construction the flag-off render. An earlier proximity-based version of that check passed while the action row was ungated, which is exactly why it was replaced.

**Decision 2 — "Include players" writes the SHIPPED pin store, not a new parameter.** The first draft passed a `requireAssets` route param. Nothing consumed it: it was a switch that would have silently done nothing. `useFinderTargets` — #186's "build around this side" — already IS the must-include contract the engine honours, and `packageMode` is what makes it literal (2+ give pins ⇒ the served card's give side carries every one). Include-OFF calls `clear()`, because a stale pin re-applies the constraint the user just switched off.

**Decision 3 — column mode MOVES the price, never drops it.** Standing the two rosters side by side halves the row to ~165pt. Dropping the trailing value column is the obvious way to win that width and is silent data loss; the value rides the row's second line instead, and the guard fails if it disappears. No type size was reduced — the Chalkline 11pt floor holds, and the two 15%-width action cells are icon-only (real `check`/`x` icons, never the report's literal ✅, which the design system forbids) with 44pt tap heights and accessibility labels.

**Decision 4 — the tour takes a HOLD, not a per-step claim.** The plan priced ruling 10 as building a suppression gate across six surfaces. `useInterruptCoordinator` already was that gate, live behind `ux.prompt_arbiter`. The genuine gap was narrower: the arbiter's slot is per-surface and frees the instant a step ends, so **in the gap between two tour steps a waiting interstitial legitimately wins it**. A tour-long `tourHold` closes that; `isInterruptBusy()` extends it to the two root modals that self-defer. Four in-flow notices that were never arbiter surfaces take a tour-only mute instead of being enrolled — enrolling them would change ordinary behaviour by making them defer to each other, far beyond what was asked.

**Decision 5 — tour beats decline behavioural retirement, and say why.** Every new beat must declare retirement. There is no client receipt meaning "this person now understands the calculator", so inventing one would be dishonest; the beats decline it with a written reason and are bounded by a display cap of 3. Only n11 retires on a receipt, because setting an outlook is literally what it asks for.

**Two rulings cost nothing, and that is worth recording.** The send-button ruling needed **no code** — `resolveSendPlatform` already routes Sleeper/MFL/ESPN. And "removal and add a player as buttons" was already satisfied by `TradeSide`'s existing add button and per-row remove. Both were verified rather than rebuilt.

**The demo removal is NOT behind this flag** and is unconditional. "Demo" names two unrelated systems across sixteen files: the demo CALCULATOR (deleted) and the demo SESSION — `/api/session/demo`, try-before-you-sync, `onboarding.demo_bridge` — which is open-access onboarding and was left untouched. `check-demo-calc-removed.js` is deliberately two-sided so a future change cannot delete the wrong one.

**Known-unbuilt, and the operator should decide it:** §6b of the plan. The ruling "this replaces the manual calc tab and lives within the league calc" collides with #310 ("we should not lock the manual calculator behind trades") and with the tour's own first beat, which needs a non-league place to start from. Built to the stated assumption — two tabs, `Manual` | `In league`, demo deleted, the rich spec on the league side — with the assumption written down rather than buried.

**Status:** Built dark on `feat/calc-finder-merge`; not merged, not pushed. **No runtime evidence exists** — the 27-step TestFlight checklist is unrun, and under [D-056](DECISIONS.md) it is the only runtime proof this can get.

**Amended 2026-08-22 (W5)** — two claims in the body above are corrected rather than rewritten, because what they got wrong is the useful part. (1) **Decision 3's ✓ cell is UNWIRED and stays that way.** The action row's confirm button is `disabled={!onLikeTrade || !bothSides}` and nothing passes `onLikeTrade`; there is no route that queues a hand-built package for a counterparty, because the deck's like path needs a server-minted `trade_id` and a canvas has no card. The cell is real, accessible and permanently disabled pending an API contract — logged as **Q-029**, and the checklist now tests that it *is* disabled instead of telling the operator to tap it. Beat n15 still describes it as working. (2) **The ✕→overlay is scoped by ORIGIN, not by the flag.** W2 read `calc.merged_layout` inside `TradeCard`, which gave the overlay to every deck for every user — the opposite of round-2 ruling 1 ("this calculator only"). W5-D made `reasonsAsOverlay` a host-set **prop**, gated on the flag **AND** `deckOrigin === 'calculator'` derived from `FinderHandoff.origin`, cleared on league switch / pins emptied / mode switch. The shipped decks keep their inline tiles. §6b remains open and is now **Q-028**.


## D-151 — The Merged Calculator Keeps Its Own Tab; "Replaces the Manual Calc Tab" Is Closed as Built

**Date:** 2026-08-22 · **Status:** decided (operator) · **Closes:** [Q-028](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md)

**Decision:** the merged surface (#384) stays the **In league** tab beside **Real values** — two tabs, as W0–W5 built it — "for now". Round-2 ruling 3 ("replaces the manual calc tab and lives within the league calc") is read as "the merged layout is what the In-league tab now is", not "one calculator page, no tabs".

**Why:** (1) #310 asked for a calculator that needs no league, and **Real values** is that reachability — collapsing to one page would re-answer #310 in the opposite direction. (2) The tour's opening beat n10 ("Tap In league…") is an `action` beat that only advances on the real tab switch; one page means a new opening beat. (3) "For now" — the operator said the eventual direction is still one surface; this is the test of it, not the end state.

**Consequences:** plan §6b is closed; the e2e review's traceability row "§6b still open" is resolved; the one-option mode row for zero-league users (review P2 #14) stays a known gap. Revisit when Find a Trade itself is absorbed (the operator's "eventually will replace it").

## D-152 — The ✓ Cell Queues a Hand-Built Package by Writing the Deck's Own Like, and Refuses Up Front

**Date:** 2026-08-22 · **Status:** decided (operator approved the contract) — **the refusal half PARTIALLY REVERSED 2026-08-31 by [D-170](#d-170--the--always-records-the-like-and-a-hand-queued-like-always-surfaces-removes-d-152s-refusal-contract-narrows-d-096-to-non-hand-built-likes)** (the ✓ now always records; only `not_league_member` still refuses) · **Closes:** the ✓ half of [Q-029](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) · **Built:** #384 W6-A, `POST /api/trades/queue`

**Decision:** the operator's spec — *"queue this trade for the other manager; it shows up in their suggested trades if it meets their preferences"* — names two systems that already exist and adds no third. "Queue this trade" **is** the deck's like: the route records a `trade_decisions` row through `_reconstruct_swipe_card` → `record_decision` → `save_trade_decision`/`save_trade_swipes`, the same path `/api/trades/swipe` uses, so the row is indistinguishable downstream. "Shows up in their suggested trades" **is** the likes-you injector, which reads exactly those rows. The blocker Q-029 recorded ("a canvas has no server-minted `trade_id`") dissolves with a deterministic id — `calcq_<sha1(user|league|opponent|sorted give|sorted receive)[:16]>` — which is also the idempotency key: a re-tapped ✓ is one row and one Elo signal, never two.

**The load-bearing choice is up-front over mirror-time.** Every gate the injector applies (membership, roster actionability, the counterparty's untouchables and not-interested lists, the D-096 quality ladder under *their* stud-tax mode) is a pure function of state the request already holds, so the route evaluates the predicate **before recording anything** and answers `{queued:false, reason}` from a closed six-value enum (`CALC_QUEUE_REASONS`, a cross-client invariant). A refusal writes nothing: recording the like and letting the mirror drop it would move the caller's Elo board and park the trade in their "Awaiting them" list for an offer the other manager would never see — precisely what the operator's *if* forbids.

**Limits, stated rather than discovered:** `queued:true` means *eligible*, not *served* — the `_LIKES_YOU_CAP` slot contest, R4 dedup, the counterparty's prior swipes and the 90-day window exist only at their next generation. Mutual-match detection is deliberately not duplicated: the queued like arrives as a likes-you card and *their* swipe mints the match through the shipped path. Route, enum and the client's in-flight lock all ride `calc.merged_layout`, which stays the whole rollback lever. A pre-existing likes-you property is carried, not fixed: the decision row is keyed by account id while roster checks use league id — a co-owner whose two ids differ will not be mirrored (noted in code).

## D-153 — Find a Trade Forks on the Canvas: Fairness-Only Packages, Not a Model Run

**Date:** 2026-08-22 · **Status:** decided (operator) · **Closes:** the second half of [Q-029](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) (by retiring its premise) · **Built:** #384 W6-B, `POST /api/trades/fair-packages`

**The rulings, verbatim:** *"this type of request shouldn't go through our models. It should be a much simpler set of cards solving for fairness only. Similar to how we determine the consolidate and downgrade suggestions already."* · On "Include players" OFF: *"C works"* — the toggle is dropped, the canvas is the anchor. · On the tour: *"the tour mentions they can add players and we'll find trades with those players included, and then the user is pushed to find a trade with the calc empty. That way the tour ends with them in the modeled cards."*

**The contract.** Find a Trade with an **empty canvas** runs the modeled deck, unchanged (the #330 hand-off auto-run). Find a Trade with **assets on the canvas** runs one synchronous sweep — no job, no divergence, no lanes, no position semantics: the canvas give side is an **exact anchor** (every card gives away precisely what the user built; nothing added) and only the return is enumerated, 1–3 assets from one partner or every league-mate, owned picks injected through the shared helper. **The receive side is a ranking preference, not a filter** — ideas containing all of it sort first, but a return that cannot include it is still shown, because an empty deck is a worse answer than a ranked one. That is why `pinned_receive_mode:'all'` is not built: there is no pinned model job for it to be added to.

**Gates shared, not copied.** `_generate_asset_ideas_impl`'s `_eval` closure became `trade_service.eval_consensus_package`; both surfaces call it, so a fair package and an Upgrade/Lateral/Downgrade idea can never price the same trade differently. The grouped enumerator was deliberately **not** generalised — its position-centric semantics and headliner ordering serve none of this caller and sit under a golden test; `generate_asset_ideas` is byte-identical. Each idea carries a deterministic `fairpk_` card id (W6-A's `calcq_` reasoning) so disposition, the ✕ overlay, the ✓ queue, flag and edit-in-calculator all work on them unchanged, and a re-swipe is one row.

**What went with the toggle:** its beat (n17) and n14 (Clear — disabled on an empty canvas, so spotlighting it was the defect W5 fixed), the `calc_include_players_toggled` registration (no emitter, no plan), and `calc_find_a_trade_tapped.include_players` → `path ∈ fair|model`. Fair decks get a "Search all trades" exit (`deck_search_all_tapped`) instead of the pin-based unpin-retry. The tour is 13 beats and ends on an empty canvas, which is what lands the user in the modeled cards. The calculator's `ScrollView` now announces scroll (`notifyGuideTargetsMoved`) and a guard makes that mandatory for any screen registering guide targets inside a `ScrollView`.

**Known limit:** the fair sweep runs from the `finderHubOn && finderMode` choke point, so with `trades.finder_hub` off (it is on) a fair hand-off lands on an empty deck — the same R-8 posture as the model auto-run.

## D-154 — The Opponent Sweep Is Complete Under `trade.full_sweep`; a Generation Budget Is Never a Deck Cap

**Date:** 2026-08-22 · **Status:** decided (operator) · **Built:** dark, branch `claude/full-sweep-0822-a1c3` · **Opens:** the latency question in [Q-030](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md) (c) · **Plan:** [`docs/plans/full-sweep/`](../docs/plans/full-sweep/plan.md)

**The ruling, verbatim (2026-08-22):** *"Aren't the agents generating all potential trades first and then presenting trades by best fit/score? And isn't [rotating the order] potentially risking point in time opportunities?"* — the operator rejected partner-order rotation as the fix and chose generate-everything-then-rank.

**What was true before.** Both opponent loops (`_generate_trades_impl`, `_generate_trades_v2`) `break` once `len(new_cards) >= global_target = max(30, max_per_opponent × 6)`. Partners after the break are never scored. In a 12-team league the sweep stops at a median **6 of 11**; because the order is ranked-first then roster order, a single-board league skips the **same** partners every refresh — 5 of 13 and 7 of 13 leaguemates never appeared across 6–11 decks ([second-read report](../docs/reviews/2026-08-22-trade-model-second-read.html) §03). `_dedup_and_sort` already ranks every collected card globally; the break was the only thing stopping that rank from covering the league.

**The decision.** Flag `trade.full_sweep` (ships `false`). ON: both breaks are skipped; ordering is unchanged and now governs streaming only; the existing sort is the global rank; `global_target` is still computed and is a flag-off stop, **never a deck cap**. OFF: byte-identical. The per-opponent keep becomes the `model_config` knob `exploration_base_per_opp` (default 5.0, reproduces the former hardcoded `_EXPLORATION_BASE_PER_OPP`) — [G-058](GOTCHAS.md). Arms `current`/`challenger`/`baseline` share the guarded loop; `gen_v2` and `fit` were verified complete sweeps already and are pinned by `test_arm_sweep_parity.py`, not changed.

**Rejected: thread parallelism.** The enumeration is pure-Python CPU work; under the GIL a thread pool buys nothing. Logged `gen_ms` (Aug): median 1.7 s, p90 5.3 s for ~6 partners; full sweep is expected ≈ 2× (p90 ≈ 10 s against a 60 s `_JOB_HARD_TIMEOUT`), and the job streams cards per partner. Only the consensus pair path has a per-pair deadline; the v3 divergence path has none (`trade_optimizer.py:231`), so the flag-on sweep carries its own wall-clock rail, `full_sweep_budget_s` (default 30 s). Real latency levers — a per-pair result cache keyed on roster/board/knob hashes, or a fork-safe process pool — are a separate phase-2 plan, deliberately not bundled here.

**Consequences accepted.** Deck size now grows toward `bakeoff_deck_limit` (code 30, prod 60) when serving is interleaved (prod: `bakeoff_serve_interleaved = 1`) and toward `exploration_base_per_opp` × partners in dark serving, plus `first_session_deck_max`; those caps are the operator's dials, not this change's. `trade.full_sweep` is process-global, so arm A sweeps too when lit — accepted, recorded in `docs/plans/three-model-bakeoff/scope-phase2.md`. The `_comment_compressed_board` "rescued boarded members displace unranked members' consensus cards" consequence is **narrowed**: with the flag on nobody is displaced. Graduation = the scope.md §3 TestFlight checklist (≥ 9 of 11 partners; `gen_ms` recorded; kill switch proven).

## D-155 — The Ram Is the Mascot and the Guide Avatar, and Its Name Is Fleeced; the Fumbling RB Is Retired

**Date:** 2026-08-22 · **Status:** decided (operator) · **Closes:** [Q-009](OPEN_QUESTIONS.md#q-009--mascot-decision-tommy-tumble-vs-ricky-rumble-vs-other) · **Brief:** [docs/plans/ram-mascot/brief.md](../docs/plans/ram-mascot/brief.md) §1

> **ID note:** originally drafted as D-154 while #384 W6-B was still uncommitted. Both reserved numbers were then taken on `main` — **D-153** by W6-B's canvas fork and **D-154** by the `trade.full_sweep` opponent sweep — so this entry and its successor were renumbered to **D-155/D-156** when the work was rebased onto `origin/main`. No gap remains; the sequence is contiguous.

**Decision:** the **ram** — the character already shipping as `mobile/assets/icon.png`, the splash, and the notification icon — is Fleeced's mascot **and** its in-app guide avatar. The "fumbling running back" concept recorded in [`../docs/design/brand.md`](../docs/design/brand.md) and [`BRAND.md`](BRAND.md) is retired unbuilt; **Q-009 closes as "neither — the ram"**, so neither Tommy Tumble nor Ricky Rumble is chosen.

**Why the docs lost, not the product.** Q-009 has been open since 2026-05-21 on the premise that the concept was settled and only the name was missing. It was not settled: an entirely different character shipped to TestFlight, to the App Store icon, and to the notification shade, while both brand docs went on describing a running back mid-fumble. This decision resolves the disagreement in favour of what users can actually see. The operator's reason is the same one: *"that avatar makes more sense to keep the branding together."*

**The character is named Fleeced.** The operator named it at D1 rather than deferring: the mascot takes the product's own name — Fleeced the ram, `Fleeced: Dynasty Trade Finder` the app ([D-057](DECISIONS.md)). The pun is the point: the ram wears the fleece, and getting fleeced is what a bad trade feels like.

Two consequences follow, and **neither is settled by this entry**:

- **The role name and the character name are now different words for the same figure.** The bubble opens *"I'm The Analyst. I model dynasty trades — you bring the roster."* Substituting the noun literally yields *"I'm Fleeced"*, which in ordinary English reads as "I just got ripped off" — the opposite of what a trustworthy guide should say in its first sentence.
